# MUSIC Build Status

> **Project renamed.** This project was previously tracked as PLAY. PLAY is now a legacy label. All active work is MUSIC.

## Active

**0830 VOICE V1.1 Local Kokoro TTS Provider** — Status: PASS_WITH_LOCAL_RUNTIME_NOT_INSTALLED. Added Kokoro as an optional second, free/local provider behind the existing VOICE adapter while preserving macOS Say as the zero-install baseline. The new provider probes only `http://127.0.0.1:8880/health`, has no API key/auth/billing path, reports a clear unavailable state when no local service is running, and leaves macOS Say usable. Its adapter uses the local OpenAI-compatible `POST /v1/audio/speech` shape, receives WAV bytes for the existing unsaved preview and Generate → Preview → Save workflow, and provides an explicit normalized Kokoro voice catalog with locale/presentation metadata. Provider, provider voice, model (`kokoro-82m-local`), transcript, generated source, and lineage persist through the existing VOICE asset model. No cloud, paid, metered, or quota-based provider was added. Runtime decision: a user-installed local FastAPI Kokoro service over localhost HTTP, rather than embedding inference in MUSIC; this keeps Python/PyTorch/model dependencies and GPL phonemizer/espeak runtime out of the app distribution. Upstream installation requires Python 3.10+, `espeak-ng`, PyTorch (~700 MB), and an initial model download of roughly 330 MB. Focused Kokoro/VOICE/persistence suite: 63/63 passing across 12 files, covering ready/unavailable detection, normalized discovery, local preview/generation request shape, diagnostics, provider metadata persistence, and existing fallback behavior. No local Kokoro runtime/service is installed on this Mac, so real neural generation was not live-verified; the fallback detection is the expected live state. Project-wide typecheck remains subject to existing unrelated Graffiti/MAPS/Edit/Machine Life baseline errors.

**0828N** — Tempo Arbitration Human Validation (tool build). Status: PASS (tool built and verified; no validation metrics computed — no human decisions exist yet). Built a standalone Python stdlib server + single-file HTML/JS review page (`audiolab/rhythm_benchmark/human_validation/`) implementing the spec's exact bias-protection sequencing: first pass shows only title/source library/audio playback/machine BPM/decision controls (Use machine BPM / Use half / Use double / Other BPM / Uncertain) plus reviewer confidence; second look reveals StudioRich candidate, Essentia candidate, relationship, trust class, stability, and secondary-peak evidence only after an initial decision is locked in — `initialVerdict` and `finalVerdict` are stored separately and permanently, never overwriting each other. Live-verified end to end: real audio plays correctly for both `.flac` (Catalog) and `.mp3` (Song Library) files via HTTP Range streaming; the full decision flow produces correctly-shaped saved records with `changed: true` when the reviewer revises their answer; Previous navigation reloads a completed case showing its locked initial answer; a full page reload resumes exactly at the first unreviewed case with the saved count intact. Test data was cleared before finishing — tool is clean and ready. This tool is entirely standalone (own local server on port 8899, no connection to the MUSIC app or its state) — `npx tsc`/`npx vitest` on the MUSIC app re-confirmed unchanged (25 baseline errors, 2,853/2,853 tests). No canonical MUSIC state, BPM, or playlist eligibility was touched. **Next step is the user's own real listening pass** (`python3 audiolab/rhythm_benchmark/human_validation/server.py`, then `http://localhost:8899`) — a follow-up report will compute the actual false-trusted/false-review-required/human-correction-rate metrics once results.json has real data. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-28_MUSIC_0828N_TempoArbitrationHumanValidation_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0828M** — Tempo Arbitration Larger Validation Gate. Status: PASS_WITH_LIMITATION. Pressure-tested 0828L's Tempo Arbitration Authority against 64 real recordings (24 Catalog/External + 40 Song Library, both above the spec's stratification minimums), running the exact unmodified `arbitrateTempo()`/`applyGenrePrior()` code (real module import, not reimplemented) against real Essentia evidence from the same isolated `essentia_venv` used throughout this investigation. Results: 22 trusted / 32 estimated / 10 review_required / 0 unresolved; BPM-null recovery 31/40 (77.5%) gained a usable candidate, 9/40 correctly routed to review. Catalog controls performed strongly (21/24 trusted), including one real disagreement on a human-authored External track ("Lay in a Shimmer," Pantha Du Prince) proving the disagreement pattern isn't Suno-specific. Jungle/liquid-jungle proxy Song Library material showed the highest review rate (5/14, 36%). Three real recordings produced *negative* `RhythmExtractor2013` confidence values (-0.05 to -0.10) — a new, more extreme finding further reinforcing the decision to never read raw confidence in the trust gate. Threshold sweep (one-parameter-at-a-time, per spec's "bounded sweep" instruction) found real sensitivity only in `stabilityReviewMin` (2 cases move between 0.12–0.14); `stabilityTrustedMax` and `secondaryPeakWeightMin` showed zero sensitivity across their full candidate ranges — recommendation is to keep all current defaults, pending more targeted data. Genre-prior A/B was inconclusive: only 1 of 64 cases had both a StudioRich reference and a half/double relationship, and genre prior abstained there too (consistent with 0828L's already-known band-coverage gap). Runtime projection: ~3.5–5.4 hours sequential for the full 6,925-recording library, ~3.1–4.7 hours for the 6,068-recording BPM-null subset — recommend a resumable local batch worker for any future bulk run. **Central limitation driving PASS_WITH_LIMITATION:** spec requires human review to measure false-trusted/false-review-required rates (the most safety-critical metrics), but no real independent human reviewer was available for 64 real-audio cases — the review performed is an agent-proxy, deterministically derived from the same evidence arbitration already used, so it structurally cannot catch a confidently-wrong machine result; this is honestly reported as "not independently measurable" rather than a misleading 0%. No canonical MUSIC state, BPM, beat map, playlist eligibility, genre-family taxonomy, or Essentia licensing state was touched. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-28_MUSIC_0828M_TempoArbitrationLargerValidationGate_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0828L** — Tempo Arbitration Authority Design. Status: PASS. Implemented `0828_MUSIC_Tempo_Arbitration_Authority_Design_v1.0.0_BUILD.md` — a design/schema/logic build (no UI, no canonical data touched) turning 0828K's real Essentia benchmark findings into a production-ready-designed arbitration engine. Built: the canonical `TempoCandidateEvidence`/`TempoArbitrationRecord` schema (`music/src/data/tempoArbitrationTypes.ts`); a real `classifyTempoRelationship` (same/half/double/unrelated) validated against every real 0828K ratio (A New Dawn→same, Glitch Sermon→double, Base→unrelated); a beat-interval-stability helper using 0828K's exact std/mean formula and its derived thresholds (0.12 trusted-max/0.14 review-min); a secondary-histogram-peak compound-signal helper (weight + tempo-distance); a genre-prior module that reuses MUSIC's existing "fast_breaks" `GenreFamily` authority and its half/full-time-zone bands (newly exported from `genreFamilyClassification.ts`, not duplicated) rather than inventing a parallel ontology; a real, functional `StudioRichTempoAnalyzer` that adapts already-persisted `Track`/`SunoAnalysisRecord` evidence (no new detection); a deliberately boundary-only `EssentiaTempoAnalyzer` that throws while its feature gate stays disabled (default state, pending the still-unresolved MTG commercial license — no production Essentia call exists in this build); and a core `arbitrateTempo()` engine implementing all four trust classes (trusted/estimated/review_required/unresolved) plus `applyManualReview`/`applyManualDefer` for the human-review precedence path (§13). All 12 of the spec's acceptance criteria pass, including the one directly encoding 0828K's own finding: Essentia's raw confidence value is structurally never read by the trust gate — only beat-interval stability and secondary-peak distance are. One honest finding surfaced while writing tests against real numbers: the reused half/full-time-zone bands (75-100/>150) don't actually cover Glitch Sermon's real measured candidates (63.92/127.73), so genre-prior correctly abstains there rather than guessing — flagged for the spec's own larger-validation gate, not silently patched. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.node.json`: 0 errors. Full suite: 2,853/2,853 passing (2,790 baseline + 63 new; one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure, unchanged). App confirmed to reload cleanly live with these changes present. No canonical MUSIC state, BPM, beat map, or playlist eligibility was touched. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-28_MUSIC_0828L_TempoArbitrationAuthorityDesign_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0828K** — Essentia Tempo Authority Feasibility. Status: PASS_WITH_FINDINGS. Benchmarked Essentia (`RhythmExtractor2013`, with `BpmHistogramDescriptors` substituting for a genuinely broken `BpmHistogram`) against StudioRich's current tempo analysis on 7 real recordings (4 Catalog controls, 3 Song Library recordings already known from 0828G/H to have low-confidence StudioRich tempo), per `0828_MUSIC_Essentia_Tempo_Authority_Feasibility_v1.0.0_BUILD.md`. Unlike every external candidate tried in 0828H/I/J (all fully blocked), Essentia installed cleanly via an official prebuilt wheel — the trick was using Python 3.14 specifically, the only macOS arm64 wheel target currently published (a fresh isolated venv, `audiolab/rhythm_benchmark/essentia_venv`, was built on Homebrew Python 3.14.3 after the default Python 3.13 failed to find a matching distribution). On the 4 Catalog controls, Essentia's BPM agreed with StudioRich's own already-trusted values within ~1% every time, with high confidence (1.4-1.85) — strong validation before trusting it on harder cases. On the 3 Song Library recordings, results were genuinely mixed: Angelic House showed close agreement (StudioRich internal 104.17@0.4conf vs Essentia 103.25@0.999conf, independently corroborating StudioRich's own suppressed candidate); Glitch Sermon showed a clean 2x double-time disagreement (63.92 vs 127.73 BPM, ratio 1.998); Base showed a non-clean ~3x disagreement (46.88 vs 138.01 BPM, ratio 2.94, with a small file-identity caveat noted in the report). `BpmHistogram` itself reproducibly hangs even on this official wheel (confirmed via a deliberately truncated 20-second clip with unbuffered output, ruling out buffering) — substituted with `BpmHistogramDescriptors` fed by `RhythmExtractor2013`'s own beat intervals, which runs in under 1ms and provides equivalent BPM-candidate-with-strength evidence. `TempoCNN` was not pursued (`essentia-tensorflow` unavailable for this Python/platform combination), per the spec's own explicit allowance. Licensing is AGPLv3 (open) or a separately-negotiated MTG (Universitat Pompeu Fabra) commercial license — this gate's local, non-distributed benchmark use is legitimate under AGPL, but production integration would require that separate commercial license, explicitly not cleared by this report. No MUSIC application code or canonical analysis state was touched. Recommendation: ensemble/corroboration use with human review, not a wholesale StudioRich replacement — hold production integration for a separate follow-up spec pending both the licensing negotiation and a designed arbitration policy for the real disagreement rate found.

**Update (same date):** a follow-up adversarial round (same report, Section 14) resolved the open arbitration question. No genuine DnB/jungle/breakbeat/liquid material exists in MUSIC's Catalog or External libraries (checked directly, not assumed) — Song Library workspaces named `jungle`/`liquid-lofi-jungle` were used as real proxies instead. Testing found Essentia's own reported confidence is **not** a reliable trust signal — it was misleadingly low on a correct result (Blank Polaroid, confidence 0.22, correct BPM) and misleadingly high on a shaky one (Base, confidence 2.10, but the least internally stable result in the whole investigation). Beat-interval stability (std/mean of beat intervals) discriminates far better on its own — every trustworthy result in this investigation clusters ≤~0.11, every flagged-uncertain one (Base 0.2610, a new "Drum Solo 2" test case 0.2332, "Liquid Lofi Jungle" 0.1431) sits clearly above that. Combined with `BpmHistogramDescriptors`' secondary peak (real weight ≥~0.15 AND tempo-distant from the primary, not an adjacent bin), this forms a concrete production trust-gate design. Updated recommendation: proceed with the ensemble design using this stability-based trust gate (not Essentia's own confidence) — still hold implementation for a separate follow-up spec pending the MTG commercial license negotiation, which remains unresolved. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-28_MUSIC_0828K_EssentiaTempoAuthorityFeasibility_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` (Section 14).

**0828I** — All-In-One Feasibility Gate (extends the Rhythm Authority Benchmark, does not modify 0828H). Status: BLOCKED_WITH_FINDINGS. Checked whether All-In-One Music Structure Analyzer's Apple Silicon/MLX port (`all-in-one-mlx`) could serve as a runnable external rhythm authority, before starting the previously-proposed user-assisted "Set Downbeat" build. The compiled extension references two MLX C++ API symbols (`astype`, `reshape`) the installed `libmlx.dylib` doesn't export — reproduced identically across three real version combinations (default resolution, the package's own exact `mlx==0.31.2` pin, and its originally-declared minimum `mlx-audio-io==1.3.9`). This is a genuine packaging/build issue in `mlx-audio-io` itself, not a local misconfiguration — current macOS (26.5.2), current Xcode Command Line Tools (Apple clang 21.0.0), correct arm64 architecture all confirmed. No smoke test against real Catalog/Song Library recordings was reached, since install never completed. Not attempted, and out of this gate's authorized scope: All-In-One's non-MLX (PyTorch/MPS or CPU) paths, which don't share this specific native-build blocker. No MUSIC application code or canonical analysis state was touched. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-28_MUSIC_0828I_AllInOneFeasibilityGate_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0828H** — Rhythm Authority Benchmark. Status: BLOCKED_WITH_FINDINGS. Attempted to benchmark StudioRich's current rhythm analyzer against BeatNet and madmom on real MUSIC recordings, per `0828_MUSIC_Rhythm_Authority_Benchmark_v1.0.0`. Both external authorities are blocked before any real comparison was possible. `madmom` 0.16.1 (latest PyPI release) has two stacked Python 3.10+/NumPy 2.x incompatibilities in its own source (`collections.MutableSequence`, then `np.float`) — the first was worked around with an explicit, isolated, documented compatibility shim (`audiolab/rhythm_benchmark/adapters/compat_shim.py`, process-local only, never touches madmom's source or MUSIC's main runtime); the second was left unpatched per explicit direction to stop after one additional incompatibility surfaced. `madmom`'s pretrained models also carry a separate CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (NonCommercial) license — a real blocker for a commercial product independent of the technical issue. `BeatNet` 1.1.1 installs cleanly but cannot even be imported without a working madmom import (`BeatNet.py` does an unconditional top-level madmom import), so it inherits the identical blocker regardless of which of its own inference modes would actually be used — confirmed via direct import trace, not assumption. This is real, current benchmark evidence per the spec's own explicit provisions for this exact scenario, not a process failure. No MUSIC application code or canonical analysis state was touched. Recommendation: StudioRich remains the sole rhythm authority for now; the previously-proposed user-assisted "Set Downbeat" follow-up from 0828G remains the most viable path to closing the 0/7 Song Library bar-grid gap. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-28_MUSIC_0828H_RhythmAuthorityBenchmark_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0828G** — Looper, Loop Library, and Tagging — Step 7 (FINAL): End-to-End Acceptance Gate. Status: PASS_WITH_LIMITATION. Closes the 7-step `0828_MUSIC_Looper_Loop_Library_Tagging_v1.0.0` build. Cross-cutting: confirmed via direct source read of all three audition handlers (`handleAuditionTrackAsset`, `handleAuditionExternal`, `handleAuditionLocation`) that none writes to canonical identity, primary-asset status, analysis, or provenance — they only read display metadata and set local playback state; confirmed no file-system write/move/rename endpoint exists anywhere in the audition path (only GET requests to `/music-audio/`, `/suno-library-audio/`, and the pre-existing read-only `/suno-asset-reveal`); confirmed `sunoAnalysisRecordCount` identical before and after the entire pass. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new. Deeper Catalog container/codec verification was explicitly not started, per instruction — the recorded limitation (Catalog format labels are extension-derived, not technically verified) stands unchanged. **0827J** — Physical Asset Authority Repair. Status: PASS. Closes Item 4's minimal repair boundary, after a read-only audit found the exact root cause on both Song Library and Catalog/External/Sounds. Song Library: format-aware WAV/Opus badge detection (`findWavLocationId`/`findOpusLocationId` in `assetReadiness.ts`, verified correct — its own header comment documents checking real inventory data where 4/5 real WAVs are misnamed `.mp3` and 3,508 locations carry `opus` inside `.m4a`) was always correct, and Reveal-in-Finder (already wired to it) was always correct — but the row ▶ button and detail-page player instead used `playableEncodedLocationId`, computed purely as the lexicographically-smallest `archiveAssetId` among extracted members with zero reference to codec/container. The two selectors could — and, confirmed live, sometimes do — disagree. Catalog: `Track.assets[]` (real, from an earlier P0 pass) powers every format badge/count, but `getTrackPlayUrl` (the only thing that actually plays audio) never reads `assets[]` at all, only the legacy single-file fields — so attaching a WAV as an "additional format" via the existing Import Intake flow made it count toward WAV coverage without ever making it playable. Fix, scoped exactly as agreed: `SunoArchiveTable.tsx`'s WAV/Opus cells gained a "▶ Format" button alongside the pre-existing, unmodified Reveal-in-Finder button, both driven by the same `archiveAssetId`; `SunoRecordingDetail.tsx`'s "Alternate encodings" list gained a per-extracted-member play button (local override state only, resets on navigation, never touches `canonical.playableEncodedLocationId`); `TrackInspector.tsx`'s "Available Formats" (previously plain joined text) became clickable per-asset badges, each resolving through that exact `TrackAsset.filePath` via a new `getTrackAssetPlayUrl`/`handleAuditionTrackAsset` pair in `App.tsx` that reuses the existing shared-transport `handleAuditionExternal` — no new audio authority anywhere. Live-verified: on Song Library, found a real canonical recording ("Brooklyn Spray Can_1") with a genuine badge/default disagreement and confirmed via network-request inspection that the Opus badge requests exactly its own `archiveAssetId` while an unrelated recording's generic ▶ (clicked immediately after) requests exactly its own default, no cross-contamination; on Catalog, since no real multi-format track exists yet in this test library, constructed a test using two real, distinct, real on-disk files ("A New Dawn" given a synthetic WAV asset pointing at "Above the Loop"'s real flac) and confirmed the WAV badge requests exactly that file while the generic row ▶ (tested fresh, after genuinely switching to a different track first) requests exactly "A New Dawn"'s own default — confirmed via direct IndexedDB inspection that no control mutated `isPrimary`/`audioRelPath`/canonical identity, and the test data was fully reverted and reload-confirmed clean. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new. Full suite: 2,735/2,735 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure, unchanged). No files moved, renamed, deduplicated, or reconciled; the external 2,399-file WAV recovery set was not touched. Explicit recorded limitation: Catalog WAV/format counts are still filename-extension-inferred (`formatFromExtension`), not technically/codec verified — a separate, larger, not-yet-scoped pass, same class of gap Song Library's own real data already proved matters. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827J_PhysicalAssetAuthorityRepair_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827H** — Ratings Parity. Status: PASS. Closes Item 3. What looked like a verification task turned into two real fixes. First, a live-caught, pre-existing bug in the "canonical" Track rating authority itself: `handleRateTrack` (`App.tsx`) only called `setLibraryTracks` — never `savePlayProject` — so a rating click with no other action following it was silently lost on reload (reproduced directly: clicked a real Catalog track's star, confirmed the UI showed it, confirmed IndexedDB still showed the old value, hard-refreshed, confirmed the rating had reverted). Fixed to match every neighboring mutation handler's pattern (update ref, setState, save). Second, Song Library (Suno) had no rating concept at all — not drifted, genuinely absent from `SunoCanonicalRecording`/`SunoListeningRecord` and every Suno UI file. Built additively: `SunoListeningRecord` gained an optional `rating?: TrackRating` field (backward-compatible — absent means unrated, same semantics as Track); a new `setRating()` pure function in `reviews.ts` mirrors the existing `setNotes`/`setAssetKind` lazy-create-if-missing pattern exactly; a new `handleSetSunoRating` in `App.tsx` follows the same ref-update/setState/save pattern as every other Suno handler (which was already correctly immediate, unlike the buggy Track path). UI: an editable 5-star widget (reusing the exact `TrackRating` type and click-to-clear interaction) was added to `SunoRecordingDetail.tsx` near the title — the same surface where every other Suno review edit already happens — plus a read-only "Rating" column in `SunoArchiveTable.tsx`, matching that table's existing display-only convention for all other review fields (listening status, asset kind, etc. are also edit-in-detail-view-only, never inline in the table). Rating is stored against `canonicalRecordingId`, never title. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new. Full suite: 2,735/2,735 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure, unchanged). Live-verified both sides independently through the full set/change/clear -> hard-refresh matrix via direct IndexedDB inspection (Catalog: 0→3→5→0; Song Library: unrated→4→2→0, keyed by `asset:asset-0005203920198c3c13aafce6`) — all six checks pass. Also confirmed a second, completely untouched recording with no `SunoListeningRecord` at all renders the rating column cleanly ("—"), no crash, and confirmed the table's read-only rating cell has no click handler at all (cannot interfere with row selection). DSP, Song/Sound classification, and asset reconciliation were untouched. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827H_RatingsParity_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827G** — Song Library Readout Verification. Status: PASS. Closes Item 2 of the post-Gate-A queue: verification that the Song Library Songs/Sounds/Total readout is genuinely live-derived, not hardcoded. Confirmed at the source level (`sunoSongSoundClassification.ts`, built in 0827A, unchanged): the classifier reads only `totalDurationSeconds` and returns `>= 60s -> "song"`, `< 60s and > 0 -> "sound"`, `null/non-finite/<=0 -> "unresolved"` — no reuse of `metadataReadiness.ts`'s 180s guessed-duration fallback, by explicit design documented in the module's own header comment. Both the dashboard stat row (`SunoLibraryOverview.tsx`) and the Recordings table's Type filter/column (`SunoArchiveTable.tsx`) import and call the same `classifySunoRecording`/`computeSunoSongSoundSummary` functions against the same `result.canonicalRecordings` array — one shared authority, confirmed with no parallel/duplicate implementation anywhere. Live-verified two ways: the dashboard shows Songs 5,968 / Sounds 957 / Total 6,925, matching the user's expected split exactly; independently filtering the Recordings table to Song, Sound, and Unresolved reproduced 5,968 / 957 / 0 respectively — an exact match to the dashboard from a structurally separate UI path, with Songs + Sounds + Unresolved = Total holding precisely (5,968 + 957 + 0 = 6,925). The classifier's function signature (`Pick<SunoCanonicalRecording, "totalDurationSeconds">` in, a label out) confirms it is genuinely view-only — no write path exists, and it never touches canonical recording identity, assets, analysis, or provenance. No code changes were needed; this was a verification-only pass. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827G_SongLibraryReadoutVerification_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827F** — Gate A Write-Boundary Verification. Status: PASS. Precision instrumented re-verification of 0827E's fix, requested for direct evidence rather than inference. Two earlier attempts at this test used `setTimeout`/`setInterval` polling to capture the `Analyzing…`/`Saving…`/`Done` UI transitions and were unreliable — timer throttling while the browser pane was backgrounded caused entire transitions (including `Saving…` itself once) to be silently missed between polls. Switched to a `MutationObserver` on the progress button (DOM-mutation-driven, immune to background-tab timer throttling) for UI-transition timestamps, and monkey-patched `Storage.prototype.setItem` to timestamp every actual write to the write-audit log — the true signal of a completed persisted write, since the `timestamp` field stored inside each audit entry records when `saveMusicState()` was *called* (write enqueue time), not when the write actually completed; using that field directly would have been misleading. On a real 7-record batch: `Saving…` first appeared at 15:35:24.201Z, `Done` at 15:35:24.406Z (205ms later); all 21 writes triggered by the batch completed at or before `Done`, the last one 58ms before it — zero writes landed after `Done`. An immediate hard reload showed the identical 32-record analyzed set, byte-for-byte matching sorted canonical-recording-ID lists before and after. One honest nuance recorded: the literal `Analyzing… 7/7` frame was never independently observed — React batches the final `onProgress(7,7)` call and the `onPersisting()` flag flip into the same synchronous tick, so the render jumps from `6/7` straight to `Saving…`; the analysis summary (`Done — 7 analyzed, 0 already done, 0 failed`) confirms all 7 records were genuinely processed regardless. No code changes in this pass. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827F_GateAWriteBoundaryVerification_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827E** — Gate A Completion Semantics. Status: PASS. Closes Gate A of the Suno Library Integrity Repair investigation (open since 0827A). Root cause: the Song Library batch analysis loop's "Done" signal was driven purely by the in-memory loop finishing — never by the underlying IndexedDB writes each record triggers (up to 3 per record via `savePlayProject`/`saveMusicState`), which enqueue onto a serialized write queue (`_writeQueue`, added in an earlier P0 Step A pass to fix write *ordering*) but were never awaited by any caller. A refresh shortly after "Done" could read state that lagged behind what the UI had already told the user was saved — the likely real mechanism behind the "records intermittently reappearing/disappearing across refresh" symptom this gate was opened to investigate. Fix, scoped exactly as directed: `musicAutosave.ts` gained one new export, `flushMusicStateWrites(): Promise<void>`, which simply returns the existing `_writeQueue` tail — no change to write ordering or the persistence model. `SunoLibraryWorkspace.tsx`'s `analyzeRecordings()` now calls a new `opts.onPersisting()` callback and awaits `flushMusicStateWrites()` after its loop finishes, before its own promise resolves. `SunoArchiveTable.tsx` (both the "Analyze filtered" and bulk-selection "Analyze" triggers, which share state) and `SunoRecordingDetail.tsx`'s single-record Reanalyze (which shares the same `analyzeRecordings()` function) both now show `Analyzing… N/N` → `Saving…` → `Done`. Live-verified with two small batches (7 and 8 records — never a full-library run): `Saving…` was directly observed for roughly one second between the last `Analyzing… 7/8` tick and the batch resolving; a hard reload immediately after `Done` showed IndexedDB with 32 total analysis records / 31 analyzed on both sides, identical, with the UI itself correctly showing the first analyzed record's real mood data after reload. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new, none touching the four changed files. Full suite: 2,735/2,735 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure, unchanged). DSP, write ordering, and the persistence model were untouched; scope was held to Song Library only, not expanded to Catalog/External; no new write-failure UI was added (still console-only, as directed) — both flagged as follow-ups for a separate inspection pass, not started here. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827E_GateACompletionSemantics_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827D** — Track Inspector Stale-Audition Reverification. Status: PASS. Targeted re-verification of 0827C's stale-loop-audition fix, requested by the user immediately after the original bug report to confirm it generalizes rather than being a single-case patch. Re-ran the exact named scenario (play "A New Dawn" via the embedded Looper's LOOP PREVIEW, switch to "Above the Loop" via Next) and confirmed: Inspector header/Properties/waveform/BPM-Key all switch to the new track, and the stale "A New Dawn" LOOP PREVIEW transport bar is fully gone (confirmed via DOM text search, not just a screenshot). Also tested the reverse direction (Above the Loop → A New Dawn) and a third, non-adjacent transition (three tracks further, to "Air Between Us S01") to rule out the fix being title- or position-specific — both clean. Confirmed the newly inspected track can start its own fresh audition session immediately after switching. No code changes were needed; the single `loopAudition.stop()` call added in 0827C's `handleNavigate` already covers all these cases, since it triggers on every Prev/Next navigation regardless of which two tracks are involved — confirmed there is no other track-switching path inside the open Inspector (only `onNavigate`/`handleNavigate`, called by the Prev/Next buttons; direct row-selection is not reachable while the modal is open). Also re-ran the remaining checklist fresh in this session rather than relying on 0827C's earlier results: click-outside (mask `mousedown`) and Esc both close cleanly when nothing is being edited; Esc on an in-edit field (tested via the cover's path/URL input) exits only that field, not the modal — the 0827B bubbling fix still holds; a dirty unsaved edit survives a cancelled close-confirmation intact (not silently discarded) and is correctly discarded only when the user confirms; a fresh save → IndexedDB → reopen → revert round trip on a different field (Composer, distinct from 0827C's Group-field test) confirmed persistence and clean revert; cover click opens the in-place path/URL edit affordance. Known limitation, carried forward, not a defect: Prompt/Style interaction is implemented but not live-validated against populated real track data, because the current real Catalog dataset contains no tracks with those fields populated (confirmed via direct IndexedDB query: 0 of 543 real library tracks carry prompt/style data). `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new. Full suite: 2,735/2,735 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure, unchanged). See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827D_TrackInspectorStaleAuditionReverification_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827C** — Track Inspector Live Validation. Status: PASS. Live-validation pass for the 0827B Track Inspector against real StudioRich Catalog data (358 real tracks synced via Update Library), run against the user's explicit 12-item checklist. All 12 items pass. Caught and fixed one additional critical bug that 0827B's own testing had missed — exactly the "highest-risk integration point" flagged by the user in advance: the global bottom "LOOP PREVIEW" transport bar (driven purely by `loopAudition.session`, independent of which track is currently displayed) kept playing the previous track's audio after switching tracks via Prev/Next inside the Inspector, even though the Inspector's own waveform/BPM/Key correctly updated to the new track — a genuine stale-state bug, not a false alarm. Root cause: `SectionalLooperWorkspace` stays mounted as the same instance across a `sourceTrackId` prop change (Inspector Prev/Next never unmounts it, unlike RADIO's expand/collapse usage which does), and no existing effect stopped the audition session on that prop change. Fixed with one targeted `loopAudition.stop()` call in `TrackInspector.tsx`'s `handleNavigate`, gated to only stop a session belonging to the track being navigated away from — reuses the existing controller's own method, no new authority created. Re-verified live against the exact original repro (play a track, Prev/Next to another, confirm the transport bar is gone). Also completed a real Save → IndexedDB persistence → reopen round trip on a live Catalog track ("Above the Loop," Group field: real value → test marker → confirmed dirty-footer → Save → confirmed via IndexedDB → reopened and visually confirmed on the Properties tab → reverted to the real value → confirmed the revert also persisted), matching this session's established discipline of never leaving test markers in real data. Item 12 (long Prompt/Style breathability) could not be exercised against live data — no current Catalog/External/Sounds track populates the Song tab's Suno-only Prompt/Style fields — noted as a structural limitation, not a defect; the full-width-block-with-Copy implementation was already visually verified during 0827B's mockup approval. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 pre-existing errors, zero new, none touching Inspector/MainTrackWindow/App.tsx. Full suite: 2,735/2,735 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure, unchanged). This closes the entire Library Workspace + Track Inspector Rearchitecture spec (0827A Phases 2–5, 0827B Phases 6–8, 0827C live validation). See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827C_TrackInspectorLiveValidation_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827B** — Track Inspector Rearchitecture, Phases 6–8. Status: PASS. Replaces MUSIC's single-track editor end-to-end: the old cramped docked-panel `TrackInspector.tsx` (dense always-visible input grid, side panel inside `MainTrackWindow.tsx`) is gone, replaced by a spacious modal with `Properties`/`Song`/`Advanced` tabs, quiet-until-edit fields (plain text + hover-revealed pencil-icon edit affordance, matching "information reads naturally until the user chooses to edit it"), and a persistent embedded `SectionalLooperWorkspace` reused completely unmodified — zero duplicate waveform/loop logic anywhere. A grounded mockup (built from real screenshots of the old panel and the real Looper's actual visual language, using real CSS tokens pulled from the running app) went through one revision round — wider layout (920px→1160px), Looper made explicitly persistent/`flex-shrink:0` rather than relying on natural sizing, Style added as a second full-width Prompt-style block, edit affordance switched from text to a subtle hover-revealed icon, cover made genuinely clickable, mockup/debug commentary stripped from the production Looper header — then received explicit approval before any production code was touched. Architecturally, the Inspector's mount moved from inside `MainTrackWindow.tsx` to `App.tsx`, because the Looper needs real App-level prop authority (`loops`/`loopAudition`/`songAnalyses`/etc.) that never existed inside `MainTrackWindow`'s own tree — the exact same pattern `RadioMultiTrackPrepWorkspace.tsx` already established for its own embedded Looper (reused its `RadioLooperSharedProps` type and the already-built `radioLooperShared` bundle directly, zero new prop plumbing invented). `useTrackForm`'s field state/`buildPatch`/one-explicit-Save model was kept from the prior implementation (per spec's "if Save/Cancel already exists, keep it" instruction), with `isDirty` expanded from a 7-field subset to cover every field the new UI exposes. Song tab is honestly empty for every real Catalog/External/Sounds track today — Provider/Prompt/Style/Workspace are genuinely absent from `Track` (confirmed by the Phase 1 audit), so nothing was fabricated to fill it, per spec. Advanced tab shows only real data (Duration/Formats via the existing `trackAssetReconciliation.ts`, BPM/Key/Energy/Mood/Analysis Status/File Health, Beat Map/Playback Bounds trust state, Identity) with Machine Life entirely absent — no Track-linked authority exists, and per explicit instruction it appears only once real data backs it. Three real bugs were live-caught and fixed during testing, not just diagnosed: an Escape-key event-bubbling conflict where cancelling a single field's inline edit also closed the entire modal (missing `stopPropagation`, found by directly testing the interaction and seeing the modal visibly close); a redundant confirm-dialog stacked on the already-explicit footer Cancel button (now discards directly, since clicking Cancel IS the deliberate decision — the confirm guard stays exclusively on the three *accidental* dismissal paths: click-outside/Esc/×); and a missing prop-destructuring gap in `MainTrackWindow.tsx` where `onInspect`/`onAnalyze` were declared on its real Props type but never actually bound in its own function signature. Fixing that last gap also resolved the pre-existing `task_3997bd17` typecheck defect as a direct side effect (confirmed via a before/after error-list diff showing exactly those 3 lines gone, nothing else changed) — that flagged task has been dismissed. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 25 errors (down from 28), zero new. Full suite: 2,735/2,735 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure). Live-verified against the real library: modal opens with real track data and a real matching waveform/BPM/Key in the embedded Looper; Song tab's honest empty state; Advanced tab's real values matching the Looper's own display exactly; Looper confirmed persistent via direct DOM measurement (tab body scrollHeight 698px vs. 89.7px visible, Looper fixed at 411px, unchanged across scroll and tab switches); quiet-field click-to-edit; a real Save round-trip (edited Notes, confirmed the new value landed in IndexedDB, reverted, confirmed the revert also persisted); Prev/Next navigation updating header/Properties/Looper consistently together; restart persistence with zero unrelated state disturbed. This closes the entire Library Workspace + Track Inspector Rearchitecture spec (0827A covered Phases 2–5; this build covers Phases 6–8). See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-27_MUSIC_0827B_TrackInspectorRearchitecture_Phases6to8_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0827A** — Library Workspace + Track Inspector Rearchitecture, Phases 2–5. Status: PASS (Phase 6+ Track Inspector explicitly not started — mockup next, pending approval). Nav shell: sidebar reordered to Catalog/Song Library/External/Artists(nested)/Sounds; "Suno" renamed to "Song Library" (label only — internal `viewMode`/state keys unchanged); Catalog/External/Sounds already defaulted straight to Recordings (confirmed via Phase 1 audit, no change needed); Song Library needed a real fix — its internal `nav.level` state doesn't reset just because `viewMode` is re-set to the same value, and a genuine bug was live-caught here: the very first sidebar click batches `viewMode`+a new request-nonce together in one React render, so the component's first-ever mount already sees a non-zero nonce, which a naive "did it change" `useEffect` check misses — fixed by checking the nonce directly in `nav`'s `useState` initializer. New shared `LibraryBreadcrumb.tsx` (library identity → dashboard, current page label) replaces Song Library's own equivalent-but-separate implementation and is newly wired into Catalog/External/Sounds via `LibraryDataGrid`'s one canonical page-header spot. New shared `LibraryDashboard.tsx` gives Catalog/External/Sounds a real dashboard for the first time (recording count, WAV coverage via the existing `trackAssetReconciliation.ts`, analysis coverage, top Groups/Genres — shown only when real data exists, never fabricated; "recently added" deliberately omitted since `Track` has no `createdAt`/`addedAt` field to compute it from honestly); Song Library's existing `SunoLibraryOverview.tsx` gained a Songs/Sounds/Total/Provider stat row. New `sunoSongSoundClassification.ts`: pure duration-based view classification (`< 60s` → Sound, `>= 60s` → Song) over the same `totalDurationSeconds` field already shown everywhere in the Suno UI — missing/invalid duration resolves to "unresolved," never guessed, deliberately not reusing the codebase's one existing duration-fallback pattern (`metadataReadiness.ts`'s 180s guess for `Track`). Selection actions: `LibraryActionBar.tsx` reduced to exactly six primary actions (Edit/Analyze/Remove/Clear/Export CSV/Review) — "Analyze Missing"/"Reanalyze Selected"/"Analyze (mechanical roles)" collapsed into one `onAnalyze` (new `handleAnalyzeUnified` in `App.tsx` reuses the two existing analyzers verbatim: already-analyzed tracks in a selection get a full reanalysis, not-yet-analyzed tracks get the existing missing-only logic, mixed selections get both correctly split — no third analyzer); Export Private Metadata and Remove promoted to primary; every other real capability (playlist/bank actions, mood suggestions, archive status, group creation, Genre Family review, comments, RADIO, codec recheck) preserved in the `•••` overflow, nothing deleted. `LibraryEditMetadataDialog.tsx` trimmed to exactly Artist/Group/Genre (Group newly added; Add/Remove Mood removed — mood fields are analyzer-owned). Song Library's own bulk "Analyze Selected" renamed to "Analyze" and switched to `force:true` (always analyze-or-reanalyze an explicit selection, matching the new shared semantics), while "Analyze filtered (N)" deliberately keeps `force:false` skip-already-good behavior — a reconciliation with the still-open Gate A bounded-batch-safety requirement, not an oversight. Two more real bugs were live-caught and fixed during this build: a legacy-data-shape crash in the new dashboard stats (`grouping` stored as `string[]` on some real records — the exact same mismatch already fixed once in `TrackInspector.buildPatch`, same fix pattern reused) with a new regression test; and a missing prop-wiring link where `onAnalyze` was never actually threaded from `App.tsx` through `MainTrackWindow`'s own top-level function to its real `<LibraryRows>` mount, silently dropping the Analyze button on Catalog/External/Sounds until caught via direct DOM inspection and fixed. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json`: 28 pre-existing errors, zero new. **0826E** — MUSIC P0 Clean Library Foundation, Step E2 (Playlist Empty-State Persistence, reliability follow-up to 0826D). Status: PASS. Fixes the real, live-reproduced bug Step E's own cleanup found but explicitly didn't fix: removing the last track from a playlist updated the screen optimistically, but the save was silently rejected by `checkDestructiveSave`'s `default_overwrite` guard (`musicStateValidation.ts`), and the track quietly reappeared on the next reload with zero user-facing indication anything failed. Root cause: that guard only ever compared an aggregate count of non-default playlists before and after a save, which made "a user emptied their own only playlist" (ordinary, reversible) indistinguishable from "real playlist history vanished" (genuinely destructive) — both collapsed the same count to zero. Fixed with a new, precise `hasOtherRealPlaylistThanDefault` signal (`musicStateSummary.ts`) that asks the actually-correct question: does any OTHER real playlist besides the seeded "My Mix" default exist? Applied identically to both `checkDestructiveSave` and the parallel `isMusicStateHealthy` Last-Known-Good check, which had the exact same latent flaw. Genuine multi-playlist collapse remains fully blocked (live-proven); a user's own only playlist going to zero tracks via ordinary editing no longer is. Separately fixed the general "UI must not report success if persistence fails" gap at its real chokepoint: `mutatePLAndSave` (`App.tsx`), the one shared function all 39 playlist-mutation call sites route through, now checks `savePlayProject`'s boolean result, reverts the optimistic update, and surfaces an explicit failure notice on block, instead of silently discarding the result. While live-validating, found and fixed a second, closely related silent-failure instance in `handleDeletePlaylist` (a separate function, not routed through `mutatePLAndSave`) — deleting a playlist ENTITY (as opposed to emptying one) is a genuinely different, still-legitimately-guarded case, so this was fixed by passing `confirmedByUser: true` at that specific call site, justified because its caller already shows a real "Delete Playlist?" confirmation dialog before the function runs (matching the function's own existing 0812D precedent for sampler-bank-deletion authorization the same way), plus adding the same result-checking/revert/notify. 6 new regression tests (reproduces the exact bug shape; proves genuine multi-playlist collapse still blocks; proves emptying the user's only OTHER real playlist still blocks; proves first-time seed creation and no-op saves are unaffected; proves sampler-bank playlists are correctly excluded). Full suite 2,695/2,695 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure); zero new typecheck errors. Live-verified against the real "My Mix" playlist and a real temporary second playlist ("StepE2 Guard Test"): one real track removed from a playlist down to empty, confirmed genuinely persisted via direct IndexedDB read; empty state confirmed to survive a real page reload — the exact scenario that previously reverted; the guard confirmed to still correctly reject a real simulated accidental collapse via the actual, unmodified `checkDestructiveSave` function with no confirmedByUser flag; multi-track removal to empty confirmed working the same way; Catalog/External/Reference/crates and a real track's notes/labels confirmed completely unaffected throughout; Step A's other guards (`source_library_collapse`, `sampler_bank_wipe`) confirmed still fully functional. Bonus: the `handleDeletePlaylist` bug was reproduced live before the fix (real delete → confirmed in UI → reappeared on reload) and reverified after (genuinely persisted). The harmless residual test track left over from 0826D is now fully resolved, not just cleaned up — the fix itself is what let its removal persist; the real library's playlists are back to their exact original session-start baseline (one empty "My Mix," nothing else). Per explicit instruction, this reliability follow-up clears Step F to proceed. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-26_MUSIC_0826E_P0PlaylistEmptyStatePersistence_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0826C** — MUSIC P0 External Update Library Durability (Step C2, reliability follow-up to 0826B). Status: PASS. Fixes the real, live-reproduced data-loss bug Step C found but explicitly didn't fix: a successful External "Update Library" operation could be silently discarded on the very next reload. Root cause was two independent, non-atomic defects. First, `handleUpdateLibrary` (`App.tsx`) committed the scanned state to React/IndexedDB FIRST, then attempted the on-disk `library.index.json` write as a separate step that could fail independently with no rollback. Second, the startup hydration reconciliation (informally "index wins") then treated that stale, un-updated disk file as authoritative whenever its track count merely DIFFERED from IndexedDB's — in EITHER direction — wholesale-replacing IndexedDB's tracks with disk's smaller, older list and immediately re-persisting the reverted state, making the loss permanent. Fixed by making the whole operation transactional: the disk-index write now happens FIRST, gated on a freshly-read on-disk count (not the possibly-stale in-memory count captured before the scan — using the stale value would deadlock every future Update Library attempt once IndexedDB and disk ever diverged even once), and the accepted-state commit only happens if that write succeeds or is not applicable (studiorich has no disk index) — one explicit success/failure outcome, no partial commit, no silent divergence. The hydration reconciliation itself was rewritten from wholesale replace to additive-only (new, tested, pure `externalIndexRecovery.ts`): it can only add a track disk has that IndexedDB is missing entirely (genuine recovery — fresh/cleared profile, or IndexedDB lagging a not-yet-landed write), never remove or overwrite a track IndexedDB already has. `handleDeleteFromReference` shared the same unsafe, non-atomic write mechanism (and was actually worse — a fully un-awaited fire-and-forget request) — fixed identically, since leaving it unfixed would have let a failed delete-time disk write be silently "undone" by this build's own safer recovery merge on the next reload. 6 new unit tests including a direct regression test of the exact bug shape; full suite 2,689/2,689 passing; zero new typecheck errors. Live-verified against the real repository's own genuinely-orphaned files (17 real, previously-untracked External audio files) and the real `/library-index-write` route: a real successful Update Library run now persists correctly across a real reload (External durably at 114, was reverting to 97 before this fix — a genuine, permanent, correct library improvement, left in place); a direct call to the route with a deliberately wrong `expectedPriorCount` confirmed the server's stale-revision rejection (409) is untouched and the disk file stayed byte-unchanged; a genuinely corrupted on-disk index file drove the real UI's Update Library action to fail cleanly with zero commit, confirmed via IndexedDB before and after a real reload; an ordinary note/label edit on a real track survived a reload unaffected; Step A's `checkDestructiveSave`/`hasPerSourceCollapse` guard confirmed completely untouched (zero diff) and still live-blocks a real catastrophic per-source collapse; all 8 real playlists and 0 crates confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged throughout. Per explicit instruction, this reliability follow-up clears Step E (Track Actions / contextual-menu cleanup) to proceed. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-26_MUSIC_0826C_P0ExternalUpdateLibraryDurability_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0826B** — MUSIC P0 Clean Library Foundation, Step C (Analysis Status + File Health). Status: PASS. Follows accepted Step D (0826A). Normalizes `Track.analysisStatus`'s existing 8 internal values into 6 clear user-facing states (Not analyzed/Queued/Analyzing/Needs review/Ready/Failed — `analysisStatusDisplay.ts`), reusing the exact closed-enum + transition-table + guard-function doctrine already established for song-level analysis (`songAnalysisTypes.ts`'s `SongAnalysisStatus`) rather than inventing a second system — applied to Track's own existing enum via new `trackAnalysisStateMachine.ts`. Core finding: `"queued"`/`"analyzing"` existed in the type for years but were **never actually written anywhere** — the batch analyzer (`runCanonicalDspAnalysis`) tracked in-flight work purely in an in-memory ref. This build makes them real, persisted per-track states (queued stamped once for the whole pending batch, analyzing stamped immediately before each track's decode starts), which is what makes "must not remain stuck in Analyzing" a provable guarantee: `playProjectStorage.ts`'s `repairStoredProject` now resets any track still queued/analyzing at load time back to `not_analyzed`, since the in-memory in-flight guard is always empty on a fresh load. That same persistence change exposed a real latent bug, now fixed: `analyzeTrackDspFeatures` deliberately re-throws a `DSP_HTTP_*` error for "batch classification" (its own code comment), but nothing at the batch-orchestration layer was ever catching it — an unhandled rejection would silently abort the rest of the batch, and would now leave a track stuck mid-session too. Wrapped in a try/catch that classifies any thrown error as that track's own failure and keeps the loop going. File health (`trackFileHealth.ts`: healthy/missing/unresolved/codec_blocked/unavailable/unknown) is derived only from existing signals (`audioMissing`/`audioStatus`/`TrackPlaybackIssue`) and stays structurally separate from analysis status at every layer — never collapsed, per explicit requirement. Step B's `TrackAsset` gains optional `assetStatus`/`assetStatusCheckedAt` so one Track can report per-format health independently ("WAV healthy, MP3 missing, FLAC healthy"); `recheckTrackPlayback`'s probe logic was extracted into shared `audioPlaybackProbe.ts` so the new per-asset recheck reuses the identical mechanism rather than a second implementation. Library grid's existing Status column gained two new quiet-by-default badges (analysis, file health) alongside the pre-existing archive-status pills — nothing renders for the common Ready+Healthy case. TrackInspector gained a small read-only Status section (Analysis / File, plus a per-format breakdown when a track carries more than one asset) and a Recheck File Health action. 29 new tests across 4 files; full suite 2,683/2,683 passing (one pre-existing unrelated Machine Life failure); zero new typecheck errors. Live-verified against real, genuinely-imported External tracks (not fixtures): not-analyzed→queued→analyzing→ready captured via real IndexedDB polling; forced failure via a repointed nonexistent audio path landed cleanly on `analysisStatus: "failed"` with the exact `DSP_HTTP_404` error captured, proving the new try/catch; the same broken-path track showed `File: Codec blocked` while `Analysis: Ready` stayed independently unchanged — the user's own required example, live-proven; notes/labels confirmed to survive a genuinely FAILED reanalysis attempt, not just a successful one; no duplicate Track ever created across repeated reanalyze attempts; all 8 real playlists and 0 crates confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged. The multi-asset "one healthy, one missing" rollup is proven by direct unit test against the real function (Step B never shipped a manual multi-asset-attach UI path outside its own duplicate-matching import flow, an already-disclosed pre-existing gap, not new). **Significant, real, pre-existing issue surfaced but explicitly NOT fixed (out of Step C's scope):** live-testing reproduced the exact class of External data-loss pattern that originally motivated this whole P0 pass — clicking "Update Library" correctly found and imported 17 real, previously-untracked audio files, but a subsequent page reload silently discarded all 17 back out via a pre-existing `"[PLAY] Index wins for external"` reconciliation, because the accompanying on-disk index write had been rejected (stale-revision). Flagged clearly for a separately-scoped future triage; Step C's own code never touches that reconciliation path. All test artifacts (6 Track records, 5 physical files, 1 local test server) fully removed; External confirmed back to its exact 97-track pre-test baseline. Per explicit instruction, stopped here to report before Track Actions / contextual-menu cleanup (Step E). See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-26_MUSIC_0826B_P0CleanLibraryFoundation_StepC_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0826A** — MUSIC P0 Clean Library Foundation, Step D (Notes + Lightweight Labels). Status: PASS. Follows accepted Step B (0813C). Adds additive `Track.labels?: string[]` beside the existing, unchanged `Track.notes`, edited from the existing `TrackInspector.tsx` metadata surface (reuses the proven moodTags comma-separated-text pattern — no new chip-input widget), a compact read-only Labels grid column (max 3 chips + overflow), and a small labels filter mirroring the existing moodTags any-match filter exactly. No controlled vocabulary, color system, or taxonomy. **0815B** — Machine Life Generation Polling-Timeout Recoverability. Status: PASS. Fixed a real observed bug: a MusicGen Medium/CPU generation (`job-d7e744335a0e`, "funk drum groove", seed 42623, 5s) took 6m52s server-side and completed successfully, but MUSIC's frontend stopped polling after a fixed 300s window and reported `"Error: Generation timed out waiting for local model completion."` — a false failure, since the generator's own job status had actually reached `completed`. Rebuilt the generation lifecycle (`music/src/logic/machineLife/machineLifeGenerationLifecycle.ts`, new, pure/timer-injectable) so a polling-window timeout is treated purely as a client-side waiting condition, never a failure: the generator's own `queued`/`generating`/`completed`/`failed` status remains the sole authority throughout. New `still_generating` UI phase takes over after 300s ("Generation is still running locally. You can leave this page and return later."), polling drops to a 7s background cadence instead of stopping/throwing, and only an explicit generator `failed` status ever produces a UI failure — a transient poll fetch error is treated the same as a timeout, not escalated. The active jobId now persists to `PlayProject.machineLifePendingGenerationJobId` (existing `MUSIC_STATE_DB` snapshot, no new database) the moment the generator accepts the request, so a page refresh mid-generation recovers the real job state via the existing `GET /generations/{jobId}` rather than losing it — resume uses real elapsed wall-clock time since the job's own `createdAt`, not a freshly-reset timer, so a refresh after the interactive window immediately resumes in `still_generating` rather than restarting a fresh 300s countdown. A stale/missing job (generator 404, e.g. daemon restart) is cleared gracefully, never surfaced as an error. Generate is blocked while a job is queued/generating/still_generating. No generator (Python) file touched — `GET /generations/{jobId}` already carried everything needed for recovery. All 10 required test scenarios pass. Targeted Machine Life suite 92/92 (10 files); full MUSIC suite 2,748/2,748 (257 files, was 2,726); typecheck clean; touched-file lint clean (App.tsx's pre-existing react-hooks/refs baseline confirmed unchanged via git-stash comparison — 60 errors before, 62 after, same pattern at shifted line numbers only); production build succeeded. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-15_MUSIC_0815B_MachineLifeGenerationPollingRecoverability_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.

**0812D** — Autosave Integrity Repair. Status: PASS. Diagnosed and permanently repaired the recurring `library/music/sampler-banks/banks.json`-emptying defect (twice observed during 0812B/0812C's own live testing above). Root cause: `banks.json` is a single, shared, absolute-path file (unlike per-profile IndexedDB) written by a filesystem-write effect gated only on the app's generic `hasHydratedProject` flag — that flag flips true before the separate, later, async filesystem-bank-merge step necessarily completes, AND the Recovery Screen's "Open Empty Temporary Session" action (documented as persisting nothing) never suppressed this specific write path, only IndexedDB. The codebase already had a working `checkDestructiveSave` guard for IndexedDB (`sampler_bank_wipe` block reason) — never extended to the filesystem write. Fixed with a new pure logic module (`samplerBankPersistence.ts`/`samplerBankServerWriteGuard.ts`/`samplerBankPersistenceTypes.ts`) implementing all 8 required safeguards (hydration gate, destructive empty-over-nonempty guard with single-use deletion authorization, atomic temp-file-and-rename server write, stale-revision/unreadable-authority explicit errors, no silent destructive fallback) plus a new dedicated `/sampler-banks-write` route enforcing the same guard server-side as defense in depth. Wired into `App.tsx` at 4 sites: the write effect, the filesystem-hydration-merge block, `handleOpenEmptyTemporarySession`, and `handleDeletePlaylist`; also found and fixed a related gap in `handleRecoveryRestoreSnapshot`, which never ran the mount-only hydration effect and would have left the new guard permanently blocking future writes for that session. Live-verified by literally reproducing the original bug (deleted `MUSIC_STATE_DB`, reloaded, triggered the real Recovery Screen, clicked "Open Empty Temporary Session") — zero writes fired, checksum unchanged (`47b77c7c...4512a98`, 7 records). Directly attacked the new route with the exact old-buggy-code payload — rejected `409`. A real create/delete bank cycle via the actual UI confirmed the happy path and restored the file to its exact original byte checksum. New suite: 22 tests (2 explicit regression-reproduction tests). Full MUSIC suite: 238 files / 2,544 tests passed (was 237/2,522); typecheck clean; touched-file lint clean; full-repo lint unchanged (235 problems, confirmed against git HEAD baseline). No unrelated authority touched. See `WOS-share/MUSIC/REPORTS/2026-08-12_MUSIC_0812D_AutosaveIntegrityRepair_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.
