Live verification on Friday, August 7, 2026 used the real on-disk RADIO package library plus current MUSIC lineage for `White Ropes` (`rtrack_000001`), `Beau Mot Plage` (`rtrack_000002`), and `Minimal` (`rtrack_000003`): a real exported `radio-manifest.json` contained `transitionFromPrevious: { family:"clean_cut", strategy:"clean_cut_hard_cut" }` only on `Beau Mot Plage` (the approved `White Ropes → Beau Mot Plage` adjacency), while the following `Beau Mot Plage → Minimal` adjacency remained `null`, proving the legacy fallback path stayed behaviorally unchanged when no valid hint existed. No audio-engine files or playback primitives changed; the export bridge reuses the existing writer and authority helper only. Status: **PASS**.

**0804G** — Playlist Eligibility Repair (source file named `0804B`, renumbered — see report header — due to a same-day collision with the unrelated Glyph `0804B` build). Depends on 0804F, shipped earlier today. Status: PASS. Repaired the crate-first playlist wizard (`NewPlaylistWizard.tsx` → `gatePlaylistCandidates` → `buildShapePlaylist`), which reported `0 eligible of 205 — skipped 8 blocked: 8 missing audio` / `"Intro" has no eligible tracks in its assigned crates.` against a real catalog. Root-cause investigation (reading the actual pipeline, not the spec's own diagnosis) found the true cause: `excludePendingImports()` (`audioReadiness.ts`, from a 2026-07 build) silently dropped every track pending canonical mood/DSP analysis from the candidate pool BEFORE `gatePlaylistCandidates` ran, with zero accounting — the message's denominator (205, pre-filter) and rejected-count (8, post-filter) came from two different populations. Every section failed identically because the pool was emptied upstream; the UI only ever showed the first section's ("Intro") warning, making a global bug look Intro-specific. The spec's own hypothesis — Intro hard-requires an exact `intro` role — did not match the code: the crate-first generator had no mechanical-role matching anywhere before this build. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion before implementing: pending-import-analysis is now a warning, not a hard rejection (tracks stay eligible by default); missing BPM/key/mood/role/DSP evidence now genuinely OMITS that dimension's contribution to scoring (renormalizing the remaining weights) rather than defaulting to a neutral 0.5 counted at full weight, per explicit per-field instruction. `excludePendingImports` removed entirely (`audioReadiness.ts` now exposes `countPendingImportAnalysis`, a pure count for the warning, never a filter) — the same fix applied at all 4 call sites (`NewPlaylistWizard.tsx` plus 3 older curve-based paths in `App.tsx`) since they all shared the one function. Built and wired `PlaylistEligibilityAudit`/`buildPlaylistEligibilityAudit` (`trackEligibility.ts`) — the full reconciled audit (considered/hardEligible/hardRejectedUnique/rejectionCounts/multiReasonRejectedCount/unresolvedMetadataWarnings/sectionCandidateCounts) the codebase had declared (`PlaylistGenerationGateAudit`) but never actually constructed. Added a soft Intro mechanical-role fallback ladder (`playlistShapeBuilder.ts`'s `introRoleTierOf`, reusing the existing `MechanicalMoodTag` taxonomy: tier 0 opener, tier 1 drift/reset/hold/plateau, tier 2 low-energy build, tier 3 any — a re-ranking only, never an exclusion) plus an opt-in `requiredMechanicalRoles` hard filter on `PlaylistShapeSection` (no new wizard UI control, respecting the shape config's own "intelligence belongs to the crate, not the playlist" doctrine). Added `PlaylistGenerationState` (`"complete"|"partial"|"blocked"`) and correctly gated `NewPlaylistWizard.tsx`'s "Save Draft Without Accepting" on it — a real, confirmed pre-existing violation (it showed even on zero-track generations) is now fixed, relabeling to "Save Partial Draft" when appropriate. Empty-crate vs. crate-exists-but-all-hard-blocked vs. crate-no-longer-exists now produce three distinct diagnostic messages instead of one generic one. `npx tsc -b` clean, 2169/2169 tests (33 new), production build clean; `bpmDetection.ts`/`keyDetection.ts`/`playlistArcBuilder.ts`/`pathOptionGenerator.ts`/`trackScoring.ts`/`slotGenerator.ts`/`playlistRepair/`/`playlistTransition/`/`playlistAnalyzer/`/`resolveCrate.ts` (already correct — crate membership is a stateless live query, no staleness bug existed) confirmed untouched. Live-verified end-to-end against the real Catalog: reproduced the exact reported shape (2-hour, 6-section, 1-crate) — went from the equivalent of "0 eligible" to `97 considered · 97 hard-eligible · 0 rejected`, correctly disclosed `5 unresolved metadata warnings: pending import analysis (eligible, not excluded)`, filled all 6 sections including Intro (`Intro fallback level used: 1`, confirming the ladder engaged against real mechanical-mood-analyzer output, not just synthetic test fixtures), generated 19 tracks (2h27m), and completed a full Accept-as-Playlist flow with correct per-section BPM/Key/Energy/Duration data — including the same track ("0670_Far_Sound...", BPM 117.45) whose accepted value was live-verified in 0804F earlier today, confirming the two builds compose correctly. Status: **PASS**.

Previously: **0804F** — BPM Authority Repair (source file named `0804A`, renumbered — see report header — due to a same-day collision with the unrelated Glyph `0804A` build). Status: PASS. Repairs BPM review so a user-selected candidate becomes the track's authoritative BPM, persists across reload/reanalysis, and clears the track from the unresolved-review list. The existing data model and write path were already mostly correct going in — `Track.bpm`/`bpmSource` was already the single canonical field pair (no second authority field needed), `reviewBpmField`/`needsBpmKeyReview` was already the one shared classifier for both the Library grid cell and the review dialog, and `onBulkUpdate` → `App.tsx`'s `handleBulkUpdateTracks` → `savePlayProject` was already the correct canonical write path (same one Genre-Family review uses). Live testing against a real, never-analyzed track in this library confirmed the simple accept-and-resolve case already worked end-to-end, but found one real, confirmed gap: per spec §6.2, the Review BPM & Key dialog should auto-close when the last unresolved row is accepted — it instead stayed open showing "0 tracks / Nothing to review." Fixed via a render-count transition tracked in `LibraryBpmKeyReviewDialog.tsx`. Also added, per explicit (previously-unmet) spec requirement: `resolveAuthoritativeBpm(track)`/`getBpmReviewStatus(track)` (`dspFeatureExtraction.ts`) as the one named selector pair other consumers (the Library grid's resolved-cell render, its column-auto-fit-width accessor, and `librarySorting.ts`'s BPM sort key) now read through, instead of each independently re-deriving or reading `track.bpm` raw; `buildAcceptedBpmPatch` as the validation gate that was missing between "candidate value clicked" and "value persisted" (previously any number, in or out of the valid `[40,240]` range, could be written with zero validation) — invalid candidates are now rejected before ever reaching `onBulkUpdate`, with a concise inline error; `normalizeBpmPrecision`/`BPM_PRECISION_DECIMALS` as the BPM precision policy the spec presumed existed but didn't (reuses the existing de facto 2-decimal convention already used elsewhere in this codebase, rather than inventing a new one); and a self-pruning duplicate-click guard on both BPM and Key accept handlers. `npx tsc -b` clean, 2136/2136 tests (15 new), production build clean; `bpmDetection.ts`/`keyDetection.ts`/`bpmTempoFamilyReview.ts`/`handleBulkUpdateTracks` and every playlist/crate/DJ-transition BPM display in `MainTrackWindow.tsx` confirmed untouched, per the spec's own "Excluded: Playlist eligibility changes, Crate BPM filters" scope boundary (`TrackTable.tsx`'s similar direct `t.bpm` read was confirmed dead code, imported nowhere, and also left alone). Live-verified end-to-end against the real track "0670_Far_Sound_The_02_01_03_00_3mb_audio": accepted a real 117.45 BPM candidate, the grid row updated immediately; the value survived an actual full page reload between testing passes (not simulated) and an actual click of the track's real Reanalyze button (confirming the pre-existing manual-source trust gate correctly protects an accepted value, unchanged by this build); Library Health showed BPM 97/97 healthy throughout, with no regressions introduced. One disclosed gap: the post-fix auto-close behavior was verified by code review and by the pre-fix live reproduction of the bug it fixes, but not independently re-confirmed live post-fix, since this library's only never-analyzed track was already consumed by the first test pass and the metadata editor correctly resists blanking a trusted BPM back into an unresolved state (a reassuring, pre-existing, unrelated protective behavior, not a bug). Status: **PASS**.

Previously: **0804E** — Glyph Notes, Event Vocabulary and Laser Layer. Status: PASS. Extends 0804D's silent-bar-spacing pulse manuscript with two new expressive layers, per `docs/glyph-audio/0804_GLYPH_NOTES_Event_Vocabulary_Laser_Layer_Spec_v0.1.0.md`: (1) a clearer audible-event vocabulary — light transient (tick), general drum (filled dot), clap-like (open ring, optional halo), accent (larger mark) — via a heuristic FFT-based classification pass (3-band energy ratios, spectral flatness, attack slope, post-onset decay, reusing the existing shared `fft.ts`) that never mutates the existing `DrumEvent`/`drumEventDetection.ts` onset detector; classification lives entirely in a new, provenance-separated `GlyphAudibleEvent` record carrying `sourceDrumEventId` back to its source onset. (2) An independent laser/synth-motion continuous layer (oscillating line or segmented beam) for resonant/high-frequency material: `other`-stem-then-full-mix source priority only (never vocals/bass), full-track frame analysis (`detectLaserActivity` never filters), with `layoutLaserFrames` as the sole activity-threshold gate — "coverage" means 100% of frames analyzed, not a continuously visible line; inactive passages render nothing, never an interpolated/decorative line through silence. Five pre-implementation corrections from direct instruction were incorporated: provenance separation, laser source-priority narrowing, color values isolated to one render-only module (`glyphColorPresets.ts`, imported only by the two render-layer files, never by analysis/classification/geometry), the coverage-vs-visibility distinction, and full analyzer/source/timestamp provenance retention on every classified event and laser result. New data: `glyphEventVocabularyTypes.ts`, `glyphLaserLayerTypes.ts`; `GlyphComposition`/`GlyphLayerVisibility` extended with optional fields only (0804C/D compositions load unchanged). New logic: `glyphEventVocabulary.ts`, `glyphEventSymbolGeometry.ts` (reuses already-computed `DrumMark` canvas points, no second placement pass), `laserSourceSelection.ts`, `laserLayerAnalysis.ts`, `laserLayerLayout.ts` (row-aware via a new `nearestPlacedPoint` on `timeToCanvasPosition.ts`, with the original function now a thin, byte-identical wrapper), `laserLayerGeometry.ts`, `glyphColorPresets.ts`. Modified: `glyphCacheKey.ts` (+12 fields), `glyphSvgExport.ts` (clap/accent/laser SVG groups, renderer v2→v3, colors now resolved via `resolveColorPalette` instead of `renderProfile.strokeColor` directly). New interface: `GlyphEventLayerEditor.tsx`, `GlyphLaserLayerEditor.tsx` (both render `null` until their data exists, per the Creative Interface Doctrine). `npx tsc -b` clean, 2121/2121 tests, production build clean; `apps/glyphlab-reference/`, `DrumEvent`/`drumEventDetection.ts`, and all protected systems confirmed untouched. A real bug was found and fixed during live verification: `GlyphFullCanvasPreview.tsx`'s outer container had a hardcoded dark (`#050505`) background regardless of color mode, making monochrome's black strokes (tuned to match the SVG export's implicit light/print background — no rect is drawn for monochrome in the exported SVG) invisible on screen; fixed by making the background conditional on `colorMode` (light paper tone for monochrome, unchanged dark chrome for cover) — a UI-chrome-only fix, no color values or analysis/geometry code touched. Live-verified against 4 real External-library tracks: "White Ropes" (real 4-stem set — laser correctly resolved to the `other`-role `instrumentalStem`, 538 drum events with a conservative 10/538 clap-like rate, 100% laser frame coverage with only 33.6% visible/non-decorative), and three full-mix-fallback tracks ("Beau Mot Plage", "Lay in a Shimmer", "Dauerläufer" — all lack stems, correctly fell back to `fullMix` for both drums and laser, with visibly noisier event counts 3–4× the isolated-stem track). All required and new invariants held on every track (`accepted event symbols = visible event symbols`, `laser coverage = 100%`, `laser dropped segments = 0`, plus the unaffected 0804C/D pulse/bar invariants). Both color modes (monochrome, cover) confirmed rendering correctly post-fix. "Frank's track" confirmed absent from this library (0/97 External, 0/88 Sounds) and disclosed rather than substituted; no track in this library was sparse enough to demonstrate a whole-track low-laser-activity example (33–65% visible-frame ratios across all 4 tracks tested), also disclosed rather than fabricated. Status: **PASS**.

Previously: **0804D** — Glyph Notes, Silent Bar Spacing and Event Dot Reassignment. Status: PASS. Narrow visual-language correction on top of 0804C, per `docs/glyph-audio/0804_GLYPH_NOTES_Silent_Bar_Spacing_Event_Dot_Reassignment_Spec_v0.1.0.md`: bars are no longer punctuated with a dot — a bar boundary now produces silent horizontal spacing (a fresh path segment/pen-lift, still inside the same continuous run and section), reserving the dot vocabulary exclusively for real audible events (drum onsets; clap/accent tiers deferred). Three corrections were incorporated before implementation per direct instruction: (1) the auto-fit layout's row-count estimate is genuinely recomputed from real gap-inflated geometry for BOTH the candidate-selection and placement phases via one shared `chunkRunIntoRows` real-accumulated-width walk (replacing the old fixed `pulsesPerRow = floor(safeWidth/pulseWidth)` count, which would have silently under-counted rows once gaps existed); (2) `GlyphLayerVisibility.barPunctuation` was made optional/deprecated rather than deleted, so a composition saved by 0804C still type-checks and loads with no migration step; (3) `PulseArchGeometry` gained `segmentBoundaryReason: "runStart"|"bar"|"phrase"|null` alongside `startsNewSegment: boolean`, giving diagnostics an independently-derived, testable bar-gap count. New `continuousGlyphRuns.ts`: `GlyphSpacingConfig`/`DEFAULT_GLYPH_SPACING` (`barBoundaryBehavior:"smallGap"`, `phraseBoundaryBehavior:"gap"`, `barGapMultiplier:1.75`, `phraseGapMultiplier:3` — reusing 0804B's `BoundaryBehavior` type vocabulary without touching 0804B's own `DEFAULT_CONNECTION_GRAMMAR`/pipeline); `buildContinuousGlyphRuns` inserts the gap and tags the boundary reason; `buildRunPathCommands` emits a fresh `M` at every `startsNewSegment` arch; `findBarPunctuationPoints` removed (dead code); new `countBarBoundaries`/`countInsertedBarGaps` back the required invariant. `fullCanvasLayout.ts`'s new `chunkRunIntoRows` is shared by `evaluateCandidate` and placement so the two phases can never disagree about row count; `barPunctuationPoints` removed, `barBoundaryCount`/`insertedBarGapCount` added. `glyphSvgExport.ts`'s bar-dot rendering removed (the `<g id="bar-punctuation">` group stays, empty, for §23 layer-order stability); `GLYPH_FULL_CANVAS_RENDERER_VERSION` bumped v1→v2. `GlyphCanvasEditor.tsx`'s now-meaningless "Bars" layer toggle removed; `GlyphDiagnostics.tsx`/`GlyphWorkspace.tsx`/`GlyphFullCanvasPreview.tsx` updated to match. `npx tsc -b` clean, 2049/2049 tests (12 net new), production build clean; `apps/glyphlab-reference/` confirmed untouched, no unrelated files touched. Live-verified end-to-end against the real track "White Ropes" (482 pulses, 120 bars): no dots anywhere, correct `mmmm  mmmm  mmmm` visual spacing, diagnostics green with `Bar boundaries 120 = Inserted gaps 120`, drum dots still independently correct (`separatedDrumStem · 538 events, 538 visible`), Save/Export SVG both clean. A genuine, unplanned improvement surfaced live: the real row-count fix found a better-fitting canvas candidate than 0804C's own estimate, eliminating the previously-disclosed `overflowRight: 8` residual entirely (now 0, with Rows 16 vs 0804C's 11). Status: **PASS**.

Previously: **0804C** — Glyph Notes, Full Canvas / Pulse Truth / Drum Layer Foundation. Status: PASS. Replaces Glyph's beat-grid-dependent pipeline (0804A/0804B) with a Pulse Truth model, per `docs/glyph-audio/0804_GLYPH_NOTES_Full_Canvas_Pulse_Truth_Drum_Layer_Spec_v0.1.0.md` — directly fixing the exact blocker 0804B hit live (`Track.beatMap.beatTimesSeconds` empty on every real library track). The full pulse grid is now derived from confirmed BPM + track duration alone (`expectedPulseCount = floor(duration*bpm/60)`); detected beats only refine phase/timing within a bounded tolerance (`secondsPerPulse*0.15`) via a circular-mean phase offset, never deciding pulse existence or count. New `glyphPulseTruthTypes.ts`/`glyphCanvasTypes.ts`/`glyphDrumLayerTypes.ts`; `GlyphComposition` now requires `pulseTruthSnapshot`/`canvasPresetSnapshot`/`viewportMode`/`layerVisibility` plus an optional `drumLayerSnapshot`. New logic: `pulsePhaseAlignment.ts`, `pulseTruth.ts` (`computePulseTruth`), `continuousGlyphRuns.ts` (one hump per pulse joined by literal shared endpoints — `arch[n].end === arch[n+1].start` — section-boundary-only breaks at the pre-layout stage), `fullCanvasLayout.ts` (auto-fit algorithm trying candidate pulse widths largest-to-smallest, falling back to the minimum width and reporting overflow honestly rather than ever dropping a pulse; each row gets its own freshly-built `PlacedGlyphRun`, so a run's path always breaks at row boundaries too, never only at section boundaries, while preserving `sectionId` across the break), `timeToCanvasPosition.ts`, `drumEventDetection.ts` (real dependency-free onset detector: high-pass emphasis → frame energy → flux → adaptive threshold → peak picking → min spacing → normalized strength; plus `selectDrumAudioSource`, the tier-1/2/3 drum source-priority selector), `drumLayerLayout.ts`. Modified: `glyphCacheKey.ts` (new, separate `computeFullCanvasCacheKey`), `glyphSvgExport.ts` (new, separate `buildFullCanvasSvgDocument` with the required `pulse-manuscript`/`bar-punctuation`/`drum-events`/`section-markers` layer-group order). New interface: `GlyphCanvasEditor.tsx`, `GlyphFullCanvasPreview.tsx` (renders the identical layout geometry the SVG exporter renders — preview/export parity by construction), `GlyphDiagnostics.tsx` (required-invariant display, showing `overflowRight`/`overflowBottom` as two separate numbers per this build's own correction pass rather than one combined value); `GlyphWorkspace.tsx` was entirely rewritten as the new primary pipeline (BPM-confirmation flow → Pulse Truth → continuous runs → full-canvas layout → drum analysis → Save/Export), deliberately NOT routing through 0804B's connection-grammar/run-formation system or 0804A's `manuscriptLayout.ts`/`archGrammar.ts` generation — those modules remain fully intact and pass their own tests but are unwired from this new pipeline, a disclosed scope cut. A genuine invariant-violating bug was found via live verification against a real 3:55/123.05 BPM track: the original pulse-generation loop (`while (gridTime < duration)`) is NOT equivalent to the `expectedPulseCount` formula whenever `phaseOffsetSeconds≈0` and duration isn't an exact multiple of `secondsPerPulse` — it systematically generated one extra pulse (482 expected vs. 483 generated), violating the spec's own required `expected=generated=placed=visible` invariant; fixed by bounding the loop to exactly `expectedPulseCount` iterations, with 4 new regression tests including the exact real-world failing case. A follow-up correction pass then fixed a second real gap: the drum source-priority selector (`selectDrumAudioSource`) was fully built and unit-tested from the start but never actually called — `handleAnalyzeDrums` always hardcoded `source:"fullMix"`. Now wired end-to-end via a new `selectDrumAudioSourceForTrack`, reading the existing stem archive read-only (`fetchStemSets`, the same GET the Sectional Looper already uses) and decoding a real drum stem, when one exists at `"current"` lifecycle, through the existing session-only `stemLooperSource.ts` adapter — no stem-system changes. A real dual-shape bug (`track.audioRelPath` vs. `filePath`) was caught and fixed mid-wiring by reusing the existing `resolveTrackAudioIdentifier` helper. `npx tsc -b` clean, 2037/2037 tests (90 new since 0804B), production build clean; `apps/glyphlab-reference/` confirmed untouched, no `packages/` created, no unrelated MAPS/itinerary/orb/overlay/vehicle/render/stem-library file touched. Full live verification succeeded end-to-end against the real track "White Ropes" (Soulphiction, 3:55, real 4-stem stem set): BPM-confirmation flow, exact pulse-count invariant match (482=482=482=482, coverage 99.9%, overflowRight 8/overflowBottom 0), Save, Analyze Drums (correctly resolved to `separatedDrumStem`, 538 real onset events, confirmed via a real `/stem-sets`+`/stem-set-asset?...role=drums` network round-trip), and Export SVG all worked with zero real console errors. Status: **PASS**. One disclosed residual, not a defect: a small honest `overflowRight: 8` (≈0.8mm) on this specific track/canvas combination — the auto-fit algorithm's best candidate still leaves a small sliver, reported honestly per §12 rather than hidden; a real tuning opportunity for a future build, not required to be zero by the spec's own design.

Previously: **0804B** — Glyph Notes, Connection Grammar. Status: PARTIAL_PASS. Adds the Connection Grammar layer to Glyph (`docs/glyph-audio/0804_GLYPH_NOTES_Connection_Grammar_Spec_v0.1.0.md`), inserted into the pipeline exactly where the spec requires: `Beat Units → Mapping → Pulse Arch Generation → Connection Grammar → Glyph Run Formation → Manuscript Layout → SVG Export`. A pulse arch is now the smallest visual unit; adjacent pulse arches join into deterministic connected runs via an explicit `decideConnection`/`buildGlyphRuns` stage that produces real `GlyphRun[]`/`ConnectionDecision[]`/`ConnectionWarning[]` data — never implicit export-time behavior. Default grammar matches the spec's §11 preset exactly: `withinSection` mode, bar boundaries punctuate (dot), phrase boundaries widen (gap), section boundaries break, silence gets an extended gap, connectors use a soft-sag curve. New data: `glyphConnectionTypes.ts` (full type set) plus `connectionGrammarId`/`connectionGrammarSnapshot`/`connectionOverrides` added to `GlyphComposition`. New logic: `connectorGeometry.ts` (canonical, non-DOM endpoint derivation + 5 connector-path styles + a shared `placeEndpoints` transform), `punctuationGeometry.ts` (dot/dotCluster/gap/restMark, deterministic spacing), `connectionGrammar.ts` (the full decision function — chronology guard, manual override, hard section break, silence, mode-ceiling-adjusted boundary behavior, geometry guards, connector build — 28 unit tests), `glyphRunFormation.ts` (explicit run formation + diagnostics — 18 unit tests). Modified logic: `glyphCacheKey.ts` (connection grammar now participates in the deterministic cache key), `manuscriptLayout.ts` (punctuation/break-driven spacing, switched from fixed-column math to a running cursor so spacing is real distance, not overlap — backward-compatible), `glyphSvgExport.ts` (draws connectors/punctuation between same-row adjacent glyphs, never across rows). Modified interface: `GlyphPreviewCanvas.tsx` (renders connectors/punctuation via the exact same geometry functions the SVG exporter calls), `GlyphExportPanel.tsx` (threads connection data through to export — a necessary addition beyond the spec's own "suggested" file list, without which exports would never include this layer), `GlyphWorkspace.tsx` (live connection-grammar draft state, calls `buildGlyphRuns` as an explicit pipeline stage, diagnostics line). New `GlyphConnectionEditor.tsx` exposes the spec's exact v1 control set (connection mode, bar punctuation, break-at-section, connector style, gap size, dot size). A real engineering correction made mid-build, documented in `connectionGrammar.ts`'s header comment: the geometry guards (distance/baseline/collision) were redesigned away from literal cross-frame coordinate comparison once tests exposed that two independently-generated glyphs' local endpoints aren't in a shared coordinate space before layout runs — replaced with span/baseline-based compatibility checks, deterministic and fully tested. `npx tsc -b` clean, 1947/1947 tests (79 new), production build clean; `apps/glyphlab-reference/` confirmed untouched and no unrelated MAPS/itinerary/orb/overlay/vehicle/render/stem/library files touched. PARTIAL_PASS solely because no track in this session's library has ever had a beat grid detected (`Track.beatMap.beatTimesSeconds` empty on every checked track, including after running Analyze) — a pre-existing gap unrelated to this build — so the full connected-preview/export flow could not be live click-tested against real audio; nav/routing and the no-beat-grid fallback were confirmed live against a real External-library track with zero console errors.

**0729A** — Review Dialog Simplification. Status: PASS. UI-reduction-only pass on both Catalog review dialogs, on top of 0728H. Review Genre Family is now a compact Track/Evidence/Fast Breaks table with exactly two decisions — plain `Yes`/`No` buttons, no repeated "Fast Breaks" label — Yes confirms `fast_breaks`, No confirms non-fast-break while preserving whatever family already existed, neither leaves the track unresolved. Confirm Fast Breaks/Keep Existing Family/Edit Manually/Defer/Reject Suggestion/the detailed-genre dropdown are all removed. Review BPM & Key is now Track/BPM/Key, offering only the real candidate values as one-click buttons (no "Use" prefix, no Edit manually, no Leave unresolved); a track with no analysis yet shows `Not analyzed` + one `Analyze` action reusing the exact existing `onAnalyzeMissing` path scoped to that single track; a track analyzed with nothing retained shows `No result` and no buttons. Manual text-entry is gone from both dialogs entirely (main library inspector's job now). All technical detail (confidence, reasons, evidence text, tempo family, genre, alternates) moved behind a small per-cell `▸ details` disclosure, keeping the default row free of paragraph text; both dialogs widened to a 760px compact table. Play/Pause unchanged from 0728H. No classifier, BPM/key detection, playback, or persistence logic touched anywhere — every write still goes through the same `onBulkUpdate`/`writeClassification`/`acceptBpmValue`/`acceptKeyValue` paths, and Analyze reuses the app's existing analyzer function verbatim. Live-verified end-to-end against real Catalog tracks: Canyon Transit s01's real conflict (63%, ⚠) resolved via No (confirmed non-fast-break, family preserved, BPM unchanged); Concrete Pulse's real agree case (80%) resolved via Yes (confirmed fast_breaks); Static Pulse's already-trusted BPM correctly showed no buttons while its low-confidence Key showed 4 real candidates (accepted 8A); a genuinely never-analyzed track ran through a fresh Analyze click end-to-end, resolving live in the dialog to real candidate values. 1735/1735 tests unchanged (UI-only, no logic file touched), typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728H** — Review Dialog Playback and Audio Fast-Break Evidence. Status: PASS. Two-part follow-up to 0728G. Part 1: added a shared `LibraryReviewRowPlayButton` next to every track title in both Review BPM & Key and Review Genre Family dialogs, reusing the app's single existing playback engine (`auditionTrackId`/`playbackStatus`/`onAuditionTrack`/`onPauseTrack`/`onResumeTrack` — the same props the Catalog grid row and bottom transport already share); no second audio element; closing a dialog mid-playback pauses and cleanly hands control back to the transport (position preserved). Part 2: added `fastBreakAudioEvidence.ts`, a narrow rhythm-evidence scorer reusing only existing DSP outputs (`onsetDensity`, `zeroCrossingRate`, `bpmConfidenceDetail.metricalConfidence`; reuses the existing "percussive-fragments" threshold pair rather than inventing new numbers) — no new signal processing, no external service, no general ML classifier. `genreFamilyClassification.ts`'s `reviewGenreFamilyField` now combines this with 0728G's declared/metadata evidence: agree (declared + audio high/medium) → strongest suggestion; audio high alone, declared absent → candidate; declared present + audio measured low → candidate WITH a conflict warning; declared present + audio *insufficient* (no DSP yet) → declared-only, explicitly NOT a conflict (absence of measurement ≠ disagreement — a real bug where this was initially conflated was caught and fixed before live verification, since 298/405 real Catalog tracks have no DSP analysis yet). Live-verified against the real Catalog's 3 fast-breaks fixtures (the same ones from 0728G): Hallway Glow stays correctly exempt (confirmed status short-circuits before audio evidence is even consulted); Concrete Pulse's previously-rejected suggestion correctly resurfaced with a new agree-branch reason (real audio measured "medium," agreeing with its declared "Jungle" tag); Canyon Transit s01 produced a genuine real metadata/audio CONFLICT (declared "Drum and Bass" at 191 BPM vs. real audio measuring "low" rhythmic density) — the required disagreement case, on real, non-fabricated data. No "ambiguous blend" track or fresh "audio-high-alone" real example exists in this Catalog; both disclosed as gaps, not fabricated. BPM confirmed unchanged before/after all interactions. 1735/1735 tests (16 new), typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728G** — Fast Breaks Identification. Status: PASS. Added a normalized `fast_breaks` genre-family classification, distinct from mood ("Ambient mood ≠ Ambient genre family"). New `Track.genreClassification` field, manual > imported > derived > unresolved precedence mirroring `bpmSource`/`keySource`. New `genreFamilyClassification.ts` is evidence-only (real genre tokens or explicit title/grouping/comment text; mood/BPM band corroborate only, never sufficient alone). New Catalog-only "Review Genre Family…" selection-dock action mirrors "Review BPM & Key…" exactly — confirmed live absent from External's menu. Confirm/Keep Existing/Edit Manually/Defer/Reject Suggestion are the only paths to a canonical family; nothing auto-assigns. A separate BPM tempo-option row (§9 arithmetic: ×2 for 75–100 BPM, ÷2 for >150 BPM) is proven independent of genre confirmation (BPM byte-identical before/after a genre confirm). Rejected suggestions stay suppressed via a persisted evidence-signature check (only reappear if evidence changes); deferred ones correctly keep reappearing — both confirmed live across genuine dialog reopens and to survive Analyze Missing/Reanalyze Selected/reload. Crate/playlist eligibility extended minimally (confirmed-only, never a suggestion), unit-tested including the required Ambient-mood-vs-fast_breaks-family safeguard; RADIO/transition correctly left untouched (no existing genre hook). Disclosed mid-build: a stray click triggered the pre-existing "Update Library" action, growing the real Catalog from 10 to 405 tracks (non-destructive, already saved) — the audit was fully re-run against the real 405-track dataset per instruction, confirming exactly 3 real candidates exist (all self-disclosed test fixtures) and 0 additional evidence among 61 real tracks sharing the same suspicious BPM bands. 1719/1719 tests (21 new), typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728F** — BPM Tempo Family Review. Status: PASS. Added a StudioRich-specific tempo-family review heuristic to the existing Review BPM & Key dialog (0728D/0728E's own workflow, not a new subsystem). A suspicious BPM (<50 or >150, canonical or a retained low-confidence candidate) now shows a plain-arithmetic half/double-time partner (`bpmTempoFamilyReview.ts`'s `classifyTempoFamily()`, matching the spec's own worked examples exactly) plus a genre-ranked recommendation from a closed whitelist (jungle/drum-and-bass/breakcore/breakbeat → high tempo plausible; ambient/cinematic/lo-fi/downtempo/dream pop/soft electronic or unrecognized/missing genre → suggest halving, never auto-deciding; explicitly never infers hardcore techno/gabber/happy hardcore/speedcore/thrash metal). `reviewBpmField`/`needsBpmKeyReview` (dspFeatureExtraction.ts) extended so a RESOLVED track with a suspicious canonical BPM is now visible to this dialog for the first time — previously invisible once resolved — while a value already trusted (manual/embedded_metadata/csv_metadata, via the existing `classifyBpmTrust`) is permanently exempt from re-flagging regardless of its number. Use/Keep both write through the exact same accept path already established in 0728D. Per instruction, a real-Catalog audit was returned BEFORE any BPM mutation: this 10-track test library has only 2 tracks below 50 BPM and zero above 150, with zero genre metadata anywhere — the spec's requested live-verification scale (5 low/5 high-ambient/3 high-DnB) was not achievable against real data, disclosed explicitly rather than fabricated. Live-verified fully: Hallway Glow's double-time candidate (80.12) accepted, Static Pulse's value (49.22) explicitly Kept, both surviving Analyze Missing, Reanalyze Selected, and reload. High-BPM/genre branches (same code path) covered by 15 exact-match unit tests instead. Zero changes to bpmDetection.ts, keyDetection.ts, any threshold, or key handling. 1698/1698 tests (24 new), typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728E** — Catalog Single-Track Remove. Status: PASS. Added a real per-row `Remove from Catalog…` action — previously a single Catalog row exposed only `Excl.`, with no way to remove one track without first multi-selecting via the bulk selection dock. Replaced the lone `Excl.` button with a compact `•••` row menu (`Exclude from analysis`/`Restore` + `Remove from Catalog…`, folded together rather than adding a second permanent control). Reuses the EXISTING bulk-removal confirmation dialog and handler byte-for-byte: the row's Remove item sets the same `removeConfirmIds` state (`LibraryDataGrid.tsx`) the selection dock's bulk action and the pre-existing Delete/Backspace shortcut already set — one deletion path, not two. The confirmation (`LibraryRemoveConfirmDialog.tsx`), for the exactly-one-track case only, now names the exact track and lists every crate/playlist/RADIO playlist/RADIO bank currently referencing it, via a new pure `trackReferenceReport.ts` that reuses `resolveCrate.ts`'s existing crate-matching function verbatim and resolves RADIO membership through `RadioInboxItem.sourceTrackId`/`assignedPlaylistIds`/`assignedBankIds`. Verified live with Hello Mello against all 9 required scenarios, including constructing real crate/playlist references to prove the populated case, then confirming clean auto-resolution afterward (crate recomputes to 0 automatically; playlist slot compacted with no gap by the pre-existing `handleRemove`). Disclosed but not fixed: a pre-existing autosave guard (`musicStateValidation.ts`) can block persistence of a removal that happens to empty the project's only playlist to zero slots — a latent property of the already-shipped bulk-removal path, surfaced only by this build's own thorough verification, not introduced by it. 1674/1674 tests (9 new), typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728D** — Catalog Header And BPM Candidate Review. Status: PASS. One focused Catalog correction covering three requested items. (1) Header freeze: `LibraryDataGrid.tsx`'s column header disappeared on scroll because its scroll container's parent `<div>` was never actually height-bounded — `position: sticky` had nothing to stick within (`.cat-grid-scroll` computed to full content height, ~3331px). A first attempt (`overflow-x: auto; overflow-y: visible`) was proven not to work via a live `getComputedStyle` check — the CSS Overflow spec forces a `visible` axis to compute as `auto` when its sibling axis isn't visible. Fixed by bounding the real parent (new `cat-grid-page-root`: flex column, `height:100%; min-height:0`) so `.cat-grid-scroll` becomes a genuine scroll container (`flex:1; min-height:0; overflow:auto`); `.cat-col-header { position: sticky; top: 0 }` then correctly holds through vertical AND horizontal scroll. `.mtw-scroll` (0728C's real scroll container for other libraries) needed no changes. (2) Traced the real BPM/Key detector end-to-end on 5 named tracks (Hello Mello, Night Still On s01 [accepted], Structural Resonance 02.1, Found Sound Pulse, Hallway Glow) — full candidate/half-time/double-time/confidence-detail/gate/warning-code data for each, before touching any behavior. (3) Extended `TrackAudioAnalysis` with four new review-only fields (`bpmCandidate`, `keyCandidateTonic`, `keyCandidateMode`, `keyCandidateCamelot`), computed unconditionally in `bpmDetection.ts`/`keyDetection.ts` ahead of their unchanged confidence gates — the pre-existing "accepted value" fields (`actualBpm`/`camelot`/etc., already load-bearing for `audioFeatureAdapter.ts`'s mood-analysis fallback and `importCsv.ts`) were deliberately not repurposed. `LibraryBpmKeyReviewDialog.tsx` now shows a rejected-with-candidate BPM as `Detected pulse / Half-time / Double-time / Confidence / Reason` with `Use <half>` / `Use <detected>` / `Edit manually` / `Leave unresolved`, writing through the existing manual-source trust path (confirmed live to survive Analyze Missing, Reanalyze Selected, and a full page reload). A 10-track independent calibration table (aubio cross-check) found confidence does NOT reliably rank-order actual accuracy — three low-confidence candidates (0.018–0.095) matched the independent reference within 1%, a moderate-confidence one (0.277) was 17% off, and even the single accepted (above-threshold) candidate diverged from the independent reference by 34%. Conclusion, returned before any threshold discussion per instruction: no confidence-threshold change is justified by this data. No global half/double "correction" rule exists or was added (confirmed live on Concrete Pulse: candidate 136/confidence 0.095 stays canonically unresolved, never silently halved). Hello Mello (contains lyrics, temporary calibration case) fully traced on BPM (three tools — user's external 165.4/82.7, MUSIC's own 87.59/43.8/178.21, this build's independent aubio check 126.04 — three different readings) and Key (MUSIC: D minor/7A/0.679/`KEY_MODE_AMBIGUITY`; external: "F#", confirmed via `camelot.ts` to be a genuinely different tonic, not MUSIC's own already-flagged ambiguity) — preserved in the completion report, NOT removed from the Catalog this build per explicit instruction to defer removal until calibration/verification complete. All 11 required live-verification points passed. 1665/1665 tests, typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728C** — Selection Dock Positioning. Status: PASS. Same-day positioning-only fix to 0728B: the compact selection bar rendered inline near the Catalog header, so on a long, scrolled library (verified live against a real 97-track External library) it was unreachable without scrolling back to the top. Confirmed the exact live layout path before editing: `App.tsx`'s own top-level shell (`display:flex; flex-direction:column`) already owns both the scrolling `.workspace` region and the persistent `.player-sampler-bar` (housing `<PlaybackTransport>`) as direct siblings. Rather than lifting `LibraryDataGrid.tsx`'s local selection state up through `MainTrackWindow.tsx`/`LibraryRows` (a broader change than "positioning only" licenses), added one empty anchor `<div id="selection-dock-root">` as a new sibling in App.tsx's shell, and had `LibraryDataGrid.tsx` portal its existing, byte-for-byte-unchanged `<LibraryActionBar>` into that anchor via `ReactDOM.createPortal` — zero changes to selection state, bulk-edit logic, analyzer behavior, or BPM/key review logic. The `•••` menu was flipped to open upward (the bar now sits at the bottom of the viewport) with a `maxHeight`/`overflowY` guard. `.cat-action-bar`'s `position: sticky` was removed entirely — no longer needed, since the bar's stable position now comes from the portal target's place in the app shell, not CSS. A real, disclosed discovery made while constructing the verification: `.cat-grid-scroll` (the class name implying it's the scroll container) is NOT actually the scrolling element — `.mtw-scroll` (a `MainTrackWindow.tsx`-owned ancestor) one level up is; this is pre-existing and unrelated to this build, corrected for in the verification approach rather than the code. All 10 required live-verification points passed: selected 4 of the last rows in a 97-track library scrolled to bottom, dock appeared immediately above the transport with zero additional scrolling (screenshot-confirmed); Edit Metadata and Analyze Missing both worked correctly from that position (Analyze Missing confirmed via fresh `analysisUpdatedAt` timestamps on the selected tracks); the `•••` menu measured opening upward and fully within the viewport (menu bottom ≤ button top, menu top ≥ 0), showing the complete unchanged action list; horizontal scroll left the dock's bounding rect unchanged; Clear removed the dock and emptied the anchor; the transport remained fully playable throughout (confirmed advancing) with no overlap of sidebar, scrollbar, or transport in a live screenshot. 1665/1665 tests, typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728B** — Catalog Selection Actions. Status: PASS. Same-day follow-up to 0728A, reorganizing the Catalog/External/Sounds selection toolbar (`LibraryActionBar.tsx`, the real live component confirmed in 0727A) into the compact Mixxx/Suno-style bar: `N selected · Edit Metadata · Analyze Missing · ••• · Clear`. Two analyzer-ownership questions were resolved with direct code evidence before any wiring: (1) mechanical-role completeness cannot be judged from `mechanicalMoodTags.length === 0` — `analyzeMechanicalMoods()` (mechanicalMoodAnalyzer.ts) ALWAYS stamps `mechanicalAnalysisStatus: "partial"|"analyzed"` once it runs, even when zero confident roles result, so the field's absence (`== null`) — not the tag array — is the one reliable "never attempted" signal; a "stale mechanical result" case can't be detected (no revision marker exists) and was disclosed rather than fabricated. (2) Mood analysis has its own separately-owned existing runner — App.tsx's `handleAnalyzerReviewBatchMood` already calls `analyzeTrackMood` directly, independent of the DSP pipeline — so a mood-only-incomplete (but DSP-complete) track in `Analyze Missing` is routed through a direct `analyzeTrackMood` loop, not through `runCanonicalDspAnalysis` (which would needlessly redecode audio and touch bpm/key/energy). New `handleAnalyzeMissingSelected` (App.tsx) splits the selection across all three existing, independently-owned analyzer boundaries (`requiresCanonicalAnalysis` → `runCanonicalDspAnalysis`; `needsAnalysis` (newly exported, unchanged) for the mood-only subset; `mechanicalAnalysisStatus == null` → `runMechanicalAnalysis`), sequenced to avoid a stale-read clobber. Two new dialogs consolidate existing actions: `LibraryEditMetadataDialog.tsx` (Set Artist/Genre, Add/Remove Mood — the four fields previously scattered as inline-expanding controls) and `LibraryBpmKeyReviewDialog.tsx` (lists only tracks failing a new shared `needsBpmKeyReview`; Accept estimate / Edit manually / Leave unresolved, all through the existing bulk-update path). New shared `reviewBpmField`/`reviewKeyField`/`needsBpmKeyReview` (dspFeatureExtraction.ts) give one classification reused by both the Catalog table's BPM/Key cell and the review dialog — critically, an "estimate" is shown ONLY from an already-persisted `audioAnalysis.actualBpm`/`.camelot`, never reconstructed from confidence sub-scores (bpmDetection.ts deliberately discards its own raw candidate below its confidence threshold — verified live against a track whose `actualBpm` was genuinely undefined, correctly showing no Accept option). Everything else previously in the bar (RADIO, playlist/bank, the old "Analyze"/"Reanalyze", archive status, mood suggestions, create group, recheck codec, export CSV, remove, edit comments) moved into a new ••• dropdown — nothing removed, nothing fabricated, each still hidden (not a disabled placeholder) when unsupported. All 11 required live-verification points passed against real Catalog tracks, including a deliberately-constructed "never analyzed" fixture (reset via the existing bulk-update path, since 0727A's own auto-analysis fix means a genuinely un-analyzed track no longer occurs naturally after import) correctly resolved across all three analyzers, while a real manual BPM/key value in the same selection was excluded from every subset and survived both Analyze Missing and Reanalyze Selected, plus a full page reload. 1665/1665 tests, typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0728A** — Import Intake Simplification. Status: PASS. Same-day follow-up to 0727A, reducing Import Intake to exactly: destination, file validity, duplicate detection, editable Title/Artist, and one final `Import All Ready` batch action. Confirmed the live render path first — `ImportIntakePanel.tsx` is the sole active component (verified against the same flow observed live during 0727A). Removed crate assignment (`+ Assign crate…`, `Assign Selected → crate`), per-row Recheck/Skip/Commit, `Commit All Safe`/`Skip Blocked`, the Crates column, and the Codec/Playback "OK" column. Replaced the generic reason-less "Warning" status with four meaningful states — Ready/Needs Review/Duplicate/Blocked — every non-Ready row now shows a concrete reason inline (`intakeStatusReason`, one shared composer). Title/Artist are now real editable inputs (`editItem`, synced to both `.metadata` and `.track`); Catalog imports offer a `StudioRich` artist default (blank-artist-only, never forced, never appended to titles) plus a bulk `Apply StudioRich to All`; External/Sounds preserve their existing destination-specific behavior unchanged. Editing Title/Artist reruns the SAME `detectDuplicate` resolver live (`reresolveIntakeItem` — no second detection path): a possible-duplicate or a low-confidence-parse Needs Review both clear automatically once corrected; an exact duplicate (file-identity based) requires one new minimal inline decision — `Keep Existing` (default, excluded from import) or `Import Separately` (explicitly authorizes the incoming file, verified live to actually get imported). One real defect was caught and fixed BEFORE live verification: the Needs-Review warning was originally computed from the raw filename-parse confidence flag at construction time, before the Catalog StudioRich default was applied — meaning a healthy Catalog import with an ambiguous filename would have stayed incorrectly stuck on Needs Review despite already having complete metadata; fixed by evaluating identity-completeness on the post-default metadata, reused unchanged after every edit. Implemented in exactly three files (`music/src/data/importTypes.ts`, `music/src/logic/importIntake.ts`, `music/src/ui/ImportIntakePanel.tsx`) with **zero `App.tsx` changes** — achieved by carrying the duplicate-check library snapshot on each intake item itself (`existingTracks`) rather than adding a new prop. Live-verified all required scenarios against real WAV imports: healthy Catalog file → Ready with StudioRich default; External import → genuine Needs Review clearing to Ready on edit (StudioRich correctly not forced); a live-constructed possible-duplicate clearing automatically on title correction; a real exact-duplicate re-import resolved both ways (Keep Existing stayed excluded, Import Separately genuinely got committed — confirmed by a second real track record afterward); confirmed zero crate/per-row-commit controls remain in the rendered panel. Post-import automatic DSP + mechanical analysis (0727A's fixed sequence) confirmed unchanged and still firing. 1665/1665 tests, typecheck/build clean.

Previously: **0727A** — Import Analysis Reliability and Bulk Edit. Status: PASS. A targeted correction pass (the original spec's formal per-field analysis-state architecture was explicitly rejected in favor of "find the broken handoff, fix it, expose bulk mutation, stop"), against two real defects found by live-importing real WAV files rather than by code reading alone. BPM: `bpmDetection.ts`'s confidence gate (unchanged, no evidence it's miscalibrated) correctly withholds the canonical `bpm` value below its threshold but ALSO already computes and stores `bpmWarningCodes` explaining why — the display layer just never read it, rendering the same bare "—" for "never analyzed" and "analyzed, genuinely no confident result." Fixed in `LibraryDataGrid.tsx`'s BPM/Key cell (the real, live Catalog table — reached via `LibraryDataGrid.tsx` → `LibraryActionBar.tsx`, NOT `MainTrackWindow.tsx`'s `BulkEditBar`, which was discovered mid-build to be dead code for Catalog/External/Sounds and left untouched) by reusing the existing `.est-tag` tooltip convention Energy's "~" marker already uses. Mechanical-role analysis: confirmed via live import that `runMechanicalAnalysis` was never auto-invoked on Catalog/External import commit at all (`mechanismTags: 0` across all 3 test tracks) — fixed with one line chaining it after DSP analysis in `App.tsx`'s `handleCommitImportIntake`, so the sequence is now import commit → canonical DSP analysis → mechanical analysis. Bulk editing: added Set Artist / Set Genre / Add Mood / Remove Mood to the real live `LibraryActionBar.tsx` toolbar (Remove Mood mirrors the existing genre-remove per-track-merge pattern in `App.tsx`'s `handleBulkUpdateTracks`); widened the existing "Reanalyze" button from mechanical-only to the full existing analyzer pair (`runCanonicalDspAnalysis` + `runMechanicalAnalysis`, both reused verbatim). Live-verified end-to-end against real imported audio: 3+2 real WAV imports, auto-analysis confirmed, BPM/Key display fix confirmed, reload-persistence confirmed, bulk Set Artist → StudioRich confirmed across 5 tracks, and — per explicit user-added precedence check — manually setting `bpm: 128`/`camelotKey: "8B"` on one track then running Reanalyze Selected across a 5-track selection including it left the manual values completely untouched while still refreshing that track's own `analysisUpdatedAt` (confirming the analyzer ran and correctly declined to overwrite it, not that it skipped the track). Transport/playback confirmed unaffected. 1665/1665 tests, typecheck/build clean. No new types, schemas, thresholds, or architecture — see the report's own §23 for the explicit list of what the original spec proposed and was NOT built.

Previously: **0724D** — RADIO / DJ Mode Dock, Presentation Correction. Status: PASS. Corrected 0724C's visual presentation only (architecture — `RadioPerformanceMode`, `decideNextHandoff()`, the execution primitive, mode persistence, the 11-track order, the Orbital renderer/audio-reactivity — is byte-for-byte unchanged): removed the duplicated `STUDIORICH RADIO` eyebrow and the mode-confirmation toast render; replaced the capsule/pill dock with a plain, borderless icon row (no background/border/shadow/glow); moved the real StudioRich "Scout" mark (and a real, user-supplied `favicon.ico`, replacing the generic Next.js starter icon) to sit beside the transient track title/artist, wrapped in a same-tab `https://studiorich.tv/` link (44×44 hit target, "StudioRich Home" accessible name, reusing 0724C's tap-while-dimmed-only-reveals guard); replaced the `RADIO`/`DJ` text buttons with hand-authored inline-SVG broadcast-tower/crossfader icons; restyled every control in a neutral dark-grey token system, visually subordinate to the Orbital's own cyan/green; removed the persistent horizontal volume slider, replacing it with a speaker icon plus a separate chevron trigger that opens a temporary vertical-slider popover (auto-closes ~1.5s after interaction ends, on `Escape`, or on outside focus). Generalized 0724C's shared `useTimedVisibility` controller to cover both the track card and the icon row via one delegated focus/hover interaction zone, replacing the old dock-only suppression. Two real bugs found and fixed live: a chevron hover/click race that reopened-then-immediately-closed the volume popover (fixed by making the click handler idempotent-open rather than toggle), and a CSS transition-direction bug where the disappearing fade's slow duration was living on the base rule and therefore also slowed the interaction-triggered reveal (fixed by moving the slow duration onto the fading/dim modifier classes only, and extending the reduced-motion override to match). `studiorich-orbital` committed at `5ab54de37fb3fdd1752c67abbfef6e7c432be0e5`. **Not deployed** — held for the owner's own preview review, per explicit instruction. Disclosed verification limitation: tooltip `:hover`/`:focus` and the exact post-fix reveal-speed timing couldn't be conclusively confirmed live due to this session's Browser-pane automation not reliably registering synthetic `:hover` and throttling JS timers in backgrounded tabs — the underlying CSS fix is a small, structurally unambiguous change, verified by direct inspection of the served CSS rather than a live timing capture. Also flagged, not part of this build: uncommitted `README.md`/`.nvmrc` changes found in the `studiorich-orbital` working tree that this build did not make — left untouched and unstaged. Previously: **0724C** — RADIO / DJ Mode Dock. Status: PASS. Replaced the persistent listener chrome with a restrained visual stage and a compact `[RADIO][DJ]` dock. Reused MUSIC's real `djTransitionAuthorityGate.ts` (not re-implemented) to resolve a `transitionFromPrevious` hint at publish time; one `decideNextHandoff()` boundary on the Sites side routes every track handoff — RADIO always Direct, DJ authorizes only `clean_cut`/`clean_cut_hard_cut`, silently falling back to Direct otherwise. Live-verified: two full natural 11/11-track cycles (RADIO and DJ mode) against the real published station, a fixture-proven DJ-authorized positive path (no real cross-system bridge exists yet between RadioPlaylist and PlaylistRecord's DjTransitionPlans — disclosed, not invented), keyboard-accessible mode switch that never interrupts playback, and 5 responsive breakpoints (one real overlap bug found and fixed). Fixed two real SSR hydration-mismatch bugs. `studiorich-orbital` committed at `e2622f1f2ad98704b24ae4aa3863ca963a1c7f3a`. Previously: **0724B** — RADIO Public Listener and Production Deployment. Status: PARTIAL_PASS — DEPLOYMENT_HANDOFF_REQUIRED. Implementation and full local verification are complete (listener interface, production metadata, 11-track endurance pass, accessibility/responsive checks). This session has no authenticated ChatGPT Sites deployment tooling; deployment and live-domain verification are handed off, per the spec's own instruction, to a session/workflow that has it. Exact verified source: `studiorich-orbital` commit `95ab893f0d67f831c62e454ccf7b359098bca2f5` (its own separate git repository, not part of this monorepo). Last fully completed build: **0724A** (PASS — RADIO Data Bridge / Sites Integration. Extracted the user-supplied Sites source bundle [`WOS-share/0723_RADIO_Sites_Version5_Source_v1.0.0.tar.gz`] to `studiorich-orbital/` beside `music/`, preserving `.openai/hosting.json`/`project_id` exactly. Built `music/scripts/publish-radio-to-sites.mjs` — a deterministic, hash+size-verified, atomic packaging bridge that copies a completed MUSIC RadioWebExport into `studiorich-orbital/public/radio/<slug>/v<n>/` and flips an `active.json` pointer only after a successful commit, so a validation failure or partial copy can never leave a half-replaced live package. Wired the Sites app's existing, untouched orbital `SwarmSphere`/`SignalEngine` visual to fetch and play the published package via a single-`<audio>`-element adaptation of `radioPlayerStartSequence.ts`'s proven readiness/commit/skip-and-continue pattern — no dual-deck engine ported, no finished listener interface or new transition families added this build. Live-verified against a real 11-track StudioRich-owned station [`soft-motion-radio`]: genuine first-track audible readiness, natural sequential auto-advance on track end, correct relative URL resolution, Range-request support, clean refresh behavior, and real audio-reactive orbital visualization. Production build verified via the underlying `vinext build` directly, since the project's own packaged build/install scripts require GNU `timeout` and are documented Linux-only.). Prerequisite: **0723C** (PASS — RADIO Player First-Track Silent-Start Fix. Root-caused and fixed a real playback-engine defect: `DualDeckPlaybackEngine.preload()` always leaves a deck silent by design [correct for a crossfade's incoming deck], but track 1 never goes through `executeHardCut` — the only place gain gets restored — so it played its full real duration completely inaudibly before naturally advancing to track 2. Fixed by reusing the exact sequence already proven in `usePreparedPlaybackController.ts`'s own non-crossfade handoff, extracted into a new unit-tested `radioPlayerStartSequence.ts` module, plus deck cleanup on failure, `ended`-suppression during startup, and a synchronous click-boundary lock. Live-verified all 3 real tracks audible via the full `EngineAudibleReadiness` signal set — not just an HTTP 206 — with node counts stable throughout.). Same-day prerequisite: **0723B** (PASS — RADIO One-Action Publish. Replaced RADIO's 4-step manual publication workflow with one Publish button, reusing 100% existing approve/prepare/export functions; added the spec-required rights boundary reading Track.sourceOwner/platformUse. Live-verified the failure path against the library's only real playlist — correctly blocked as external/reference_only content, previous published version preserved. Happy-path unit-verified only: this library has 0 StudioRich-owned/streaming-cleared tracks, confirmed by direct audit.). Same-day prerequisite: **0723A** (PARTIAL_PASS — RADIO Web Playback Vertical Slice. Proved RADIO playback end-to-end — Publish→manifest→audio→player — using existing infrastructure plus one new static route and one new minimal /radio-player.html page; 3 real tracks played continuously via DualDeckPlaybackEngine.executeHardCut. Blocked only on radio.studiorich.tv hosting, which has no code/access in this repo.).

| `0723C` | RADIO Player First-Track Silent-Start Fix | PASS | Chat-instructed hotfix. Track 1 of a published station never produced audio while track 2+ played correctly. Root-caused via direct code inspection: `DualDeckPlaybackEngine.preload()` always leaves a deck silent (`gain=0`) by design — correct for a crossfade's incoming deck, which only becomes audible once `executeHardCut()` restores its gain — but track 1 never goes through a transition, so nothing ever restored its gain; it played its full real duration completely inaudibly, genuinely reached `ended`, and only then advanced to track 2. Identical, already-solved case in `usePreparedPlaybackController.ts`'s own non-crossfade first-track handoff; reused that exact sequence (`setDeckGainValue(1)` → `playDeck()` → `confirmAudibleReadiness()`), extracted into a new unit-tested `music/src/radioPlayerStartSequence.ts` module. Added: deck cleanup (pause + gain-zero) on any startup failure so a failed deck can never reach `ended` in the background; an `ended`-suppression flag so a real `ended` firing mid-startup can't advance the station before track 1 is committed; a synchronous `tryAcquireStartLock` at the click-handler boundary as the actual rapid-double-click protection; and an additive `DualDeckPlaybackEngine.getDeckDiagnostics()` getter for diagnosis. Track 1 is now committed as playing only after `confirmAudibleReadiness` reports ok — never on a merely-resolved `play()` promise or an HTTP 206 alone. 12 new unit tests; live-verified against the real published station with the full audible-readiness signal set (deck gain, AudioContext state, element-playing, position-advancing) for all 3 tracks, node counts stable throughout, rapid-double-click confirmed to construct exactly one engine. 1651/1651 tests, typecheck/lint(203, baseline)/build all clean. |

Reused existing tested modules rather than rebuilding: the lossless intermediate reuses `computeFrameRange`/`extractChannelRange`/`encodePcmWav` and the same active-revision-bounds resolution `handleRenderLoop` already uses; `parseWavHeader` is reused for decode-back frame counting. ## Completed Builds — 2026-07-15 (Sample-Accurate Loop Audition and Playhead Synchronization)

| Build ID | Name | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0715F` | Sample-Accurate Loop Audition and Playhead Synchronization | PASS | Replaced the Sectional Looper's loop-audition engine — previously a plain `HTMLAudioElement` with a manual `ontimeupdate` seek-back (coarse ~4Hz cadence, imprecise `currentTime` seeks, `LoopAuditionSession.startFrame`/`endFrame` hardcoded to 0 and never populated) — with a Web Audio `AudioBufferSourceNode`-based engine using native `loop`/`loopStart`/`loopEnd` (sample-accurate in the browser's audio thread) and a real audio-clock-derived playhead (`sourceFrame = loopStartFrame + floor((audioContext.currentTime - startedAt) * sampleRate)`, wrapped, per spec §9). Four required plan corrections were applied before implementation: (1) the wrap-timing diagnostic is honestly named `LoopWrapObservation` (UI-thread observation delay/visual lag), never claiming a measured audio-thread "overshoot" that JS/rAF cannot actually observe; (2) the old "Micro Crossfade" toggle was renamed **Boundary Fade** and made off-by-default (native hard loop is the default; a gain dip on a single looping node doesn't overlap outgoing/incoming audio and isn't a true crossfade); (3) `AudioContext.resume()` happens synchronously before any decode/fetch work; (4) `LoopAuditionStartRequest` makes exact frames mandatory (never optional/back-derived from rounded seconds), and the decode cache is keyed by stable `sourceId` and capped by both entry count and approximate decoded-byte size. Live verification on the real "White Ropes" track and its real Demucs-separated drums stem observed 33 consecutive wraps with no material lateness (mean wrap-observation delay 11.05ms, max 22.49ms; mean visual-observation lag 46.03ms, max 88.90ms — both consistent with rAF's own ~16.67ms polling granularity, not audio-thread jitter), confirmed pause/resume preserve exact position for both track and stem sources, navigation persistence (bar survives leaving/returning to the looper), stop from both the looper and the persistent bottom transport, and a genuinely new defect-prevention behavior: editing a boundary while previewing correctly stops audition and requires an explicit new Preview (§11), live-verified via a real drag-selection + numeric boundary edit. An MP3 source-zero investigation on the real file found real leading (132ms) and trailing (2.9s) silence, reported honestly as an observation only — no encoder-delay causation claimed, no automatic offset applied. Media-element fallback exists and is unit-tested but was not live-forced (disclosed, not a gap). 44 new tests (646/646 total), typecheck/test/build all clean. No protected system touched. |
